score:[14.01985794752033, 6.7223904584911125]
<start_des> 
The new scoring function aims to balance between the potential for rounding and the objective function's influence. It considers features such as the fractional part of the solution value, the number of locks for rounding, the objective function value, the pseudo cost values, and the solution value in the root node relaxation. It penalizes variables that may require rounding and gives higher scores to variables with lower fractional parts, higher pseudo costs, and greater deviations from the objective function's norm. 
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False
    
    # Penalize if rounding down/up may not be feasible
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 1
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 1
    
    # Higher score for variables with lower fractional parts
    score += 1 / (candsfrac + 1)
    
    # Higher score for variables with more locks for rounding
    score += (nlocksdown + nlocksup) / 10
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher objective function value
    score += obj
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher pseudo cost values
    score += max(pscostdown, pscostup)
    
    # Higher score for variables with greater deviation from the objective function's norm
    score += abs(objnorm - obj)
    
    # Higher score for variables with non-zero entries
    score += nNonz
    
    # Higher score for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 1
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the sign of the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

score:[1.7018781577584627, 12.232053890920032]
<start_des>
The `myheurdiving` function designed here evaluates variables for the diving heuristic in MILP by considering both feasibility of rounding directions and the variable's potential impact on the solution quality. It initially penalizes variables that can be rounded in both directions, encouraging exploration of less straightforward solutions. The function calculates a base score based on the proximity of the variable to its nearest integer, giving more weight to values closer to an integer. It incorporates adjustments for the variable's influence in the constraint matrix, its effect on the objective function when rounded, and its behavior in previous solutions. Additional considerations include penalties based on pseudo costs which reflect the expected increase in the objective function due to rounding. Special emphasis is given to binary variables, recognizing their significant role in decision-making within MILP solutions. The decision to round up or down is influenced by the feasibility, the proximity to the nearest integer, and the past performance of similar actions, aiming to stabilize and enhance the quality of the heuristic solution.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Increase penalty for dual rounding feasibility to foster exploration
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        base_score = 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding up when closer to next integer
    else:
        roundup = False
        base_score = 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding down when closer to lower integer
    
    # Build the score from various factors
    score += base_score
    score += 10 * candsol  # Weigh in the current solution value
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Incorporate pseudo costs to reflect impact on objective
    score += 2 * (obj / objnorm)  # Normalize influence by objective function norm
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalize based on locks, which restrict flexibility
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Consider the influence in the constraint matrix

    # Extra scoring for binary variables due to their binary nature
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables

    # Adjustment based on comparison with root node values
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` assesses the potential rounding directions of variables in a MILP problem based on various features. It penalizes rounding directions that may limit exploration while favoring variables with lower fractional parts, higher solution values, more locks for rounding, higher objective function values, greater deviations from the objective function's norm, higher pseudo cost values, more nonzero entries, and binary variables. The function evaluates each feature to determine the overall score and the rounding direction.

</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False
    
    # Penalize if rounding down/up may not be feasible
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5
    
    # Higher score for variables with lower fractional parts
    score += 1 / (candsfrac + 1)
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher solution values
    score += candsol
    
    # Higher score for variables with more locks for rounding
    score += (nlocksdown + nlocksup) / 10
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher objective function values
    score += obj
    
    # Higher score for variables with greater deviation from the objective function's norm
    score += abs(objnorm - obj)
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher pseudo cost values
    score += max(pscostdown, pscostup)
    
    # Higher score for variables with more nonzero entries
    score += nNonz
    
    # Higher score for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 1
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the sign of the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup

score:[13.452217013109461, 3.1765584143282224]
<start_des>
The new score function, named 'myheurdiving', incorporates a comprehensive approach to evaluate variables for MILP diving heuristics. It integrates various factors such as feasibility, proximity to integer values, past impacts, objective function influence, constraint impacts, and binary variable importance. The function aims to strike a balance between exploration and exploitation, prioritizing variables that offer promising exploration opportunities while considering their potential impact on the solution quality and stability.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible to encourage exploration
    score = -10 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0

    # Emphasize proximity to integer values
    base_score = 20 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += base_score

    # Incorporate current solution value and pseudo costs
    score += candsol * 10
    score -= (pscostup if mayroundup else pscostdown)

    # Consider objective function influence normalized by objective norm
    score += 10 * (obj / objnorm)

    # Adjust based on lock numbers and variable's impact in the constraint matrix
    score -= 5 * (nlocksdown if not mayroundup else nlocksup)
    score += 3 * nNonz

    # Enhance scoring for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Adjust based on difference from root node values to favor stability
    adjustment = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5
    score += adjustment if mayroundup else -adjustment

    # Decide rounding direction based on feasibility and closeness to integer
    roundup = (mayroundup and candsfrac >= 0.5) or (mayrounddown and not mayroundup)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

score:[12.473979123146275, 4.137132320261554]
<start_des>
The new scoring function is designed to effectively guide the diving heuristic in MILP by evaluating the potential of each variable to contribute to finding a feasible and high-quality solution quickly. This function penalizes variables that can be rounded in both directions to promote exploration over exploitation. The score is computed based on several factors, including the fractional part of the variable's solution value, the solution value itself, the objective function contribution, and the number of non-zeros, among others. It also considers the pseudo-costs to prioritize directions with lower estimated degradation in the objective. Higher scores are given to variables with larger fractional parts, higher contributions to the objective, and lower pseudo-costs. The final decision on the rounding direction favors rounding up if the score is positive, enhancing the heuristic's ability to escape local optima and progress towards an integral solution.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initialize the score
    score = 0
    
    # Penalize rounding in both directions to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 5
    
    # Add score based on the fractional part - prioritize variables far from integrality
    score += 20 * candsfrac
    
    # Add contribution of the variable to the current LP solution
    score += 10 * candsol
    
    # Adjust score based on objective function contribution
    score += 15 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Avoid division by zero
    
    # Penalize based on number of locks, preferring fewer locks to encourage changes
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not mayroundup else nlocksup)
    
    # Penalize based on pseudo costs, preferring lower costs to minimize increase in objective
    score -= 10 * (pscostdown if not mayroundup else pscostup)
    
    # Consider the change from the root node solution
    score += 8 * (rootsolval - candsol) if mayrounddown else -8 * (rootsolval - candsol)
    
    # Add a small bonus for non-zero count to slightly favor sparsity
    score += 3 * nNonz
    
    # Extra boost for binary variables to focus on fixing these first
    if isBinary:
        score += 5
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the score
    roundup = score > 0
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new scoring function, `myheurdiving`, evaluates variables based on a comprehensive set of factors to facilitate effective decision-making in MILP diving heuristics. It promotes exploration by penalizing the ability to round variables in both directions, encouraging a diverse search for feasible solutions. The function considers the fractional part of the variable's solution value, aiming to prioritize variables further from integrality to foster exploration. It incorporates the variable's current solution value and its impact on the objective function, promoting exploitation of variables contributing significantly to the optimization. Additionally, penalties based on pseudo costs discourage changes that may increase the objective function value. The function penalizes variables with a high number of locks to encourage exploration and avoid premature convergence. Furthermore, it rewards variables significantly differing from their root node values, enhancing exploration by considering potential changes from initial relaxation states. Finally, it incentivizes affecting more constraints by increasing the score for variables with a higher number of nonzero entries in the constraint matrix and appropriately adjusts the rounding direction based on the calculated score threshold.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    
    # Penalize if rounding in both directions is possible to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 8
    
    # Calculate base score from the fractional part, preferring values far from integrality
    score += 10 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5) * 2)
    
    # Include the impact of the variable's current solution value
    score += 5 * candsol
    
    # Adjust based on the objective function's contribution, normalized
    score += 7 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Avoid division by zero
    
    # Include penalties based on pseudo costs to minimize objective increase
    score -= 5 * (pscostdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else pscostup)
    
    # Penalize based on the number of locks to encourage changes
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else nlocksup)
    
    # Increase score for variables significantly differing from their root node values
    score += 3 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    
    # Add a factor for non-zero count in the constraint matrix to encourage affecting more constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz
    
    # Decide rounding direction based on score threshold
    roundup = True if score > 20 else False
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

score:[2.456350234406785, 8.241409640068674]
<start_des>
The `myheurdiving` scoring function evaluates the merit of rounding decisions during the diving heuristic process in MILP by synthesizing several aspects of a variable's current state and impact on the MILP model. This function favors exploration by penalizing the option to round in both directions, thereby promoting more decisive rounding actions. The function calculates the score based on factors such as the proximity of the variable's current fractional value to the nearest integer, the contribution of the variable to the current solution, and the potential cost of rounding measured through pseudo-costs. Additional weighting is given to the influence of the variable in the objective function relative to the norm of the objective vector, and the number of constraints the variable affects. For binary variables, which are pivotal in MILP, additional importance is granted. The function also evaluates how the variable's current solution deviates from its root solution, thus integrating a historical perspective to guide present decisions. The decision to round up or down is dynamically determined based on a blend of these factors, ensuring that each rounding action is contextually optimized.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial score penalization for dual feasibility
    score = -10 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)
    
    # Update score based on calculated proximity and variable characteristics
    score += proximity_score
    score += 10 * candsol  # Contribution of the variable to the current solution
    score -= 5 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Influence of pseudo-costs
    score += 15 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Relative contribution to the objective function
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty for locks
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Weight for influence in the constraint matrix
    
    # Additional score adjustments for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5
    
    # Incorporating the root solution comparison
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The `myheurdiving` function designed here evaluates variables for the diving heuristic in MILP by considering both feasibility of rounding directions and the variable's potential impact on the solution quality. It initially penalizes variables that can be rounded in both directions, encouraging exploration of less straightforward solutions. The function calculates a base score based on the proximity of the variable to its nearest integer, giving more weight to values closer to an integer. It incorporates adjustments for the variable's influence in the constraint matrix, its effect on the objective function when rounded, and its behavior in previous solutions. Additional considerations include penalties based on pseudo costs which reflect the expected increase in the objective function due to rounding. Special emphasis is given to binary variables, recognizing their significant role in decision-making within MILP solutions. The decision to round up or down is influenced by the feasibility, the proximity to the nearest integer, and the past performance of similar actions, aiming to stabilize and enhance the quality of the heuristic solution.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Increase penalty for dual rounding feasibility to foster exploration
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        base_score = 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding up when closer to next integer
    else:
        roundup = False
        base_score = 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding down when closer to lower integer
    
    # Build the score from various factors
    score += base_score
    score += 10 * candsol  # Weigh in the current solution value
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Incorporate pseudo costs to reflect impact on objective
    score += 2 * (obj / objnorm)  # Normalize influence by objective function norm
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalize based on locks, which restrict flexibility
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Consider the influence in the constraint matrix

    # Extra scoring for binary variables due to their binary nature
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables

    # Adjustment based on comparison with root node values
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function `myheurdiving` assesses the potential rounding directions of variables in a MILP problem based on various features. It penalizes rounding directions that may limit exploration while favoring variables with lower fractional parts, higher solution values, more locks for rounding, higher objective function values, greater deviations from the objective function's norm, higher pseudo cost values, more nonzero entries, and binary variables. The function evaluates each feature to determine the overall score and the rounding direction.

</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False
    
    # Penalize if rounding down/up may not be feasible
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5
    
    # Higher score for variables with lower fractional parts
    score += 1 / (candsfrac + 1)
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher solution values
    score += candsol
    
    # Higher score for variables with more locks for rounding
    score += (nlocksdown + nlocksup) / 10
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher objective function values
    score += obj
    
    # Higher score for variables with greater deviation from the objective function's norm
    score += abs(objnorm - obj)
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher pseudo cost values
    score += max(pscostdown, pscostup)
    
    # Higher score for variables with more nonzero entries
    score += nNonz
    
    # Higher score for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 1
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the sign of the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function, named 'myheurdiving', incorporates a comprehensive approach to evaluate variables for MILP diving heuristics. It integrates various factors such as feasibility, proximity to integer values, past impacts, objective function influence, constraint impacts, and binary variable importance. The function aims to strike a balance between exploration and exploitation, prioritizing variables that offer promising exploration opportunities while considering their potential impact on the solution quality and stability.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible to encourage exploration
    score = -10 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0

    # Emphasize proximity to integer values
    base_score = 20 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += base_score

    # Incorporate current solution value and pseudo costs
    score += candsol * 10
    score -= (pscostup if mayroundup else pscostdown)

    # Consider objective function influence normalized by objective norm
    score += 10 * (obj / objnorm)

    # Adjust based on lock numbers and variable's impact in the constraint matrix
    score -= 5 * (nlocksdown if not mayroundup else nlocksup)
    score += 3 * nNonz

    # Enhance scoring for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Adjust based on difference from root node values to favor stability
    adjustment = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5
    score += adjustment if mayroundup else -adjustment

    # Decide rounding direction based on feasibility and closeness to integer
    roundup = (mayroundup and candsfrac >= 0.5) or (mayrounddown and not mayroundup)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function aims to balance exploration and exploitation in the context of MILP diving heuristics by considering multiple factors. It penalizes ambivalence towards rounding directions to encourage decisive actions. It prioritizes exploration by favoring variables further from integrality. Additionally, it incorporates the variable's contribution to the solution's value and the impact of pseudo-costs, while adjusting for the influence of the objective function and the number of locks. It also considers the deviation from the root solution, the variable's impact on constraints, and its binary nature, promoting variables with higher constraint impact and encouraging rounding up for binary variables.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalize ambivalence to foster decisive actions

    # Prioritize exploration by favoring variables further from integrality
    base_exploration = 15 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5) * 2)
    score += base_exploration

    # Consider variable's contribution to solution value and pseudo-cost impact
    score += 7 * candsol  # Value contribution to current solution
    score -= 3 * (pscostdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else pscostup)  # Cost of rounding

    # Adjust for objective function influence and locks impact
    obj_influence = 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Normalize to prevent division by zero
    lock_penalty = -2 * (nlocksdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else nlocksup)
    score += obj_influence + lock_penalty

    # Factor in deviation from root solution value
    deviation_from_root = 4 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    score += deviation_from_root

    # Enhance score for binary variables and those impacting many constraints
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.2
    score += 1 * nNonz  # Encourage affecting more constraints

    # Decide rounding direction: prefer rounding up if score is positive
    roundup = score > 0
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new scoring function is designed to effectively guide the diving heuristic in MILP by evaluating the potential of each variable to contribute to finding a feasible and high-quality solution quickly. This function penalizes variables that can be rounded in both directions to promote exploration over exploitation. The score is computed based on several factors, including the fractional part of the variable's solution value, the solution value itself, the objective function contribution, and the number of non-zeros, among others. It also considers the pseudo-costs to prioritize directions with lower estimated degradation in the objective. Higher scores are given to variables with larger fractional parts, higher contributions to the objective, and lower pseudo-costs. The final decision on the rounding direction favors rounding up if the score is positive, enhancing the heuristic's ability to escape local optima and progress towards an integral solution.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initialize the score
    score = 0
    
    # Penalize rounding in both directions to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 5
    
    # Add score based on the fractional part - prioritize variables far from integrality
    score += 20 * candsfrac
    
    # Add contribution of the variable to the current LP solution
    score += 10 * candsol
    
    # Adjust score based on objective function contribution
    score += 15 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Avoid division by zero
    
    # Penalize based on number of locks, preferring fewer locks to encourage changes
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not mayroundup else nlocksup)
    
    # Penalize based on pseudo costs, preferring lower costs to minimize increase in objective
    score -= 10 * (pscostdown if not mayroundup else pscostup)
    
    # Consider the change from the root node solution
    score += 8 * (rootsolval - candsol) if mayrounddown else -8 * (rootsolval - candsol)
    
    # Add a small bonus for non-zero count to slightly favor sparsity
    score += 3 * nNonz
    
    # Extra boost for binary variables to focus on fixing these first
    if isBinary:
        score += 5
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the score
    roundup = score > 0
    
    return score, roundup

        proximity_score = 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5
    
    # Higher score for variables with lower fractional parts
    score += 1 / (candsfrac + 1)
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher solution values
    score += candsol
    
    # Higher score for variables with more locks for rounding
    score += (nlocksdown + nlocksup) / 10
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher objective function values
    score += obj
    
    # Higher score for variables with greater deviation from the objective function's norm
    score += abs(objnorm - obj)
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher pseudo cost values
    score += max(pscostdown, pscostup)
    
    # Higher score for variables with more nonzero entries
    score += nNonz
    
    # Higher score for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 1
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the sign of the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function, named 'myheurdiving', incorporates a comprehensive approach to evaluate variables for MILP diving heuristics. It integrates various factors such as feasibility, proximity to integer values, past impacts, objective function influence, constraint impacts, and binary variable importance. The function aims to strike a balance between exploration and exploitation, prioritizing variables that offer promising exploration opportunities while considering their potential impact on the solution quality and stability.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible to encourage exploration
    score = -10 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0

    # Emphasize proximity to integer values
    base_score = 20 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += base_score

    # Incorporate current solution value and pseudo costs
    score += candsol * 10
    score -= (pscostup if mayroundup else pscostdown)

    # Consider objective function influence normalized by objective norm
    score += 10 * (obj / objnorm)

    # Adjust based on lock numbers and variable's impact in the constraint matrix
    score -= 5 * (nlocksdown if not mayroundup else nlocksup)
    score += 3 * nNonz

    # Enhance scoring for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Adjust based on difference from root node values to favor stability
    adjustment = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5
    score += adjustment if mayroundup else -adjustment

    # Decide rounding direction based on feasibility and closeness to integer
    roundup = (mayroundup and candsfrac >= 0.5) or (mayrounddown and not mayroundup)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function aims to balance exploration and exploitation in the context of MILP diving heuristics by considering multiple factors. It penalizes ambivalence towards rounding directions to encourage decisive actions. It prioritizes exploration by favoring variables further from integrality. Additionally, it incorporates the variable's contribution to the solution's value and the impact of pseudo-costs, while adjusting for the influence of the objective function and the number of locks. It also considers the deviation from the root solution, the variable's impact on constraints, and its binary nature, promoting variables with higher constraint impact and encouraging rounding up for binary variables.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalize ambivalence to foster decisive actions

    # Prioritize exploration by favoring variables further from integrality
    base_exploration = 15 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5) * 2)
    score += base_exploration

    # Consider variable's contribution to solution value and pseudo-cost impact
    score += 7 * candsol  # Value contribution to current solution
    score -= 3 * (pscostdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else pscostup)  # Cost of rounding

    # Adjust for objective function influence and locks impact
    obj_influence = 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Normalize to prevent division by zero
    lock_penalty = -2 * (nlocksdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else nlocksup)
    score += obj_influence + lock_penalty

    # Factor in deviation from root solution value
    deviation_from_root = 4 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    score += deviation_from_root

    # Enhance score for binary variables and those impacting many constraints
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.2
    score += 1 * nNonz  # Encourage affecting more constraints

    # Decide rounding direction: prefer rounding up if score is positive
    roundup = score > 0
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new scoring function is designed to effectively guide the diving heuristic in MILP by evaluating the potential of each variable to contribute to finding a feasible and high-quality solution quickly. This function penalizes variables that can be rounded in both directions to promote exploration over exploitation. The score is computed based on several factors, including the fractional part of the variable's solution value, the solution value itself, the objective function contribution, and the number of non-zeros, among others. It also considers the pseudo-costs to prioritize directions with lower estimated degradation in the objective. Higher scores are given to variables with larger fractional parts, higher contributions to the objective, and lower pseudo-costs. The final decision on the rounding direction favors rounding up if the score is positive, enhancing the heuristic's ability to escape local optima and progress towards an integral solution.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initialize the score
    score = 0
    
    # Penalize rounding in both directions to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 5
    
    # Add score based on the fractional part - prioritize variables far from integrality
    score += 20 * candsfrac
    
    # Add contribution of the variable to the current LP solution
    score += 10 * candsol
    
    # Adjust score based on objective function contribution
    score += 15 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Avoid division by zero
    
    # Penalize based on number of locks, preferring fewer locks to encourage changes
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not mayroundup else nlocksup)
    
    # Penalize based on pseudo costs, preferring lower costs to minimize increase in objective
    score -= 10 * (pscostdown if not mayroundup else pscostup)
    
    # Consider the change from the root node solution
    score += 8 * (rootsolval - candsol) if mayrounddown else -8 * (rootsolval - candsol)
    
    # Add a small bonus for non-zero count to slightly favor sparsity
    score += 3 * nNonz
    
    # Extra boost for binary variables to focus on fixing these first
    if isBinary:
        score += 5
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the score
    roundup = score > 0
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new scoring function, `myheurdiving`, evaluates variables based on a comprehensive set of factors to facilitate effective decision-making in MILP diving heuristics. It promotes exploration by penalizing the ability to round variables in both directions, encouraging a diverse search for feasible solutions. The function considers the fractional part of the variable's solution value, aiming to prioritize variables further from integrality to foster exploration. It incorporates the variable's current solution value and its impact on the objective function, promoting exploitation of variables contributing significantly to the optimization. Additionally, penalties based on pseudo costs discourage changes that may increase the objective function value. The function penalizes variables with a high number of locks to encourage exploration and avoid premature convergence. Furthermore, it rewards variables significantly differing from their root node values, enhancing exploration by considering potential changes from initial relaxation states. Finally, it incentivizes affecting more constraints by increasing the score for variables with a higher number of nonzero entries in the constraint matrix and appropriately adjusts the rounding direction based on the calculated score threshold.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    
    # Penalize if rounding in both directions is possible to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 8
    
    # Calculate base score from the fractional part, preferring values far from integrality
    score += 10 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5) * 2)
    
    # Include the impact of the variable's current solution value
    score += 5 * candsol
    
    # Adjust based on the objective function's contribution, normalized
    score += 7 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Avoid division by zero
    
    # Include penalties based on pseudo costs to minimize objective increase
    score -= 5 * (pscostdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else pscostup)
    
    # Penalize based on the number of locks to encourage changes
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else nlocksup)
    
    # Increase score for variables significantly differing from their root node values
    score += 3 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    
    # Add a factor for non-zero count in the constraint matrix to encourage affecting more constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz
    
    # Decide rounding direction based on score threshold
    roundup = True if score > 20 else False
    
    return score, roundup

<start_des>
The `myheurdiving` scoring function evaluates the merit of rounding decisions during the diving heuristic process in MILP by synthesizing several aspects of a variable's current state and impact on the MILP model. This function favors exploration by penalizing the option to round in both directions, thereby promoting more decisive rounding actions. The function calculates the score based on factors such as the proximity of the variable's current fractional value to the nearest integer, the contribution of the variable to the current solution, and the potential cost of rounding measured through pseudo-costs. Additional weighting is given to the influence of the variable in the objective function relative to the norm of the objective vector, and the number of constraints the variable affects. For binary variables, which are pivotal in MILP, additional importance is granted. The function also evaluates how the variable's current solution deviates from its root solution, thus integrating a historical perspective to guide present decisions. The decision to round up or down is dynamically determined based on a blend of these factors, ensuring that each rounding action is contextually optimized.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial score penalization for dual feasibility
    score = -10 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)
    
    # Update score based on calculated proximity and variable characteristics
    score += proximity_score
    score += 10 * candsol  # Contribution of the variable to the current solution
    score -= 5 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Influence of pseudo-costs
    score += 15 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Relative contribution to the objective function
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty for locks
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Weight for influence in the constraint matrix
    
    # Additional score adjustments for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5
    
    # Incorporating the root solution comparison
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial penalization to promote exploration
    score = -15 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0

    # Determining the rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    # Adjusting score based on variable importance and constraints
    score += proximity_score
    score += 8 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score -= 4 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Minimize pseudo costs
    score += 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty
    score += 1.5 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    # Bonus for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.3

    # Adjustment based on comparison to root node solution
    score += 3 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -3 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup

It integrates current solution values, the impact on the objective function via pseudo costs, and the variable's influence on constraints. def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Encourage exploration by penalizing dual rounding feasibility
    score = -10 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0

    # Decision on rounding direction based on fractional part of the solution
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        base_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Higher score for rounding up when closer to the next integer
    else:
        roundup = False
        base_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Higher score for rounding down when closer to the lower integer
    
    # Calculating the total score considering various aspects
    score += base_score
    score += 5 * candsol  # Influence of the current solution value
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Adjust score based on pseudo costs
    score += 1.5 * (obj / objnorm)  # Normalized objective function value impact
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalize based on locking restrictions
    score += 1 * nNonz  # Factor in the number of non-zero entries in the constraint matrix

    # Enhance the score for binary variables significantly
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Increase the weight for binary variables

    # Apply adjustments based on root node comparison
    adjustment = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 3
    score += adjustment if roundup else -adjustment

    return score, roundup

    score += 8 * (rootsolval - candsol) if mayrounddown else -8 * (rootsolval - candsol)
    
    # Add a small bonus for non-zero count to slightly favor sparsity
    score += 3 * nNonz
    
    # Extra boost for binary variables to focus on fixing these first
    if isBinary:
        score += 5
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the score
    roundup = score > 0
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new scoring function, `myheurdiving`, evaluates variables based on a comprehensive set of factors to facilitate effective decision-making in MILP diving heuristics. It promotes exploration by penalizing the ability to round variables in both directions, encouraging a diverse search for feasible solutions. The function considers the fractional part of the variable's solution value, aiming to prioritize variables further from integrality to foster exploration. It incorporates the variable's current solution value and its impact on the objective function, promoting exploitation of variables contributing significantly to the optimization. Additionally, penalties based on pseudo costs discourage changes that may increase the objective function value. The function penalizes variables with a high number of locks to encourage exploration and avoid premature convergence. Furthermore, it rewards variables significantly differing from their root node values, enhancing exploration by considering potential changes from initial relaxation states. Finally, it incentivizes affecting more constraints by increasing the score for variables with a higher number of nonzero entries in the constraint matrix and appropriately adjusts the rounding direction based on the calculated score threshold.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    
    # Penalize if rounding in both directions is possible to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 8
    
    # Calculate base score from the fractional part, preferring values far from integrality
    score += 10 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5) * 2)
    
    # Include the impact of the variable's current solution value
    score += 5 * candsol
    
    # Adjust based on the objective function's contribution, normalized
    score += 7 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Avoid division by zero
    
    # Include penalties based on pseudo costs to minimize objective increase
    score -= 5 * (pscostdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else pscostup)
    
    # Penalize based on the number of locks to encourage changes
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else nlocksup)
    
    # Increase score for variables significantly differing from their root node values
    score += 3 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    
    # Add a factor for non-zero count in the constraint matrix to encourage affecting more constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz
    
    # Decide rounding direction based on score threshold
    roundup = True if score > 20 else False
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function, named 'myheurdiving', evaluates variables for MILP diving heuristics by integrating multiple decision factors. It assigns scores based on the feasibility of rounding directions, closeness to integer thresholds, and the variable's influence on the objective function, enhanced with details like pseudo costs and locking implications. This scoring mechanism aims to balance aggressive rounding with strategic decision-making, fostering better convergence to optimal or near-optimal solutions by selecting the most promising variables for rounding, thus speeding up the MILP solving process.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Reward or penalize based on rounding feasibility
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 20  # Penalize for increased exploration possibilities
    elif mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score += 10  # Reward if only one rounding direction is feasible

    # Adjust score based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = (1 - candsfrac) * 15
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = candsfrac * 15

    score += proximity_score

    # Enhance score based on the variable's impact on the objective
    obj_impact = (obj / objnorm) * 20
    score += obj_impact

    # Include pseudo costs to influence rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Account for lock implications on variable rounding
    lock_impact = nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup
    score -= lock_impact * 3

    # Prefer variables with higher involvement in constraints
    score += nNonz * 2

    # Favor stability by comparing to root node values
    stability_bonus = 10 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    if roundup:
        score += stability_bonus
    else:
        score -= stability_bonus

    # Binary variables receive a significant boost due to their high impact
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The `myheurdiving` scoring function evaluates the merit of rounding decisions during the diving heuristic process in MILP by synthesizing several aspects of a variable's current state and impact on the MILP model. This function favors exploration by penalizing the option to round in both directions, thereby promoting more decisive rounding actions. The function calculates the score based on factors such as the proximity of the variable's current fractional value to the nearest integer, the contribution of the variable to the current solution, and the potential cost of rounding measured through pseudo-costs. Additional weighting is given to the influence of the variable in the objective function relative to the norm of the objective vector, and the number of constraints the variable affects. For binary variables, which are pivotal in MILP, additional importance is granted. The function also evaluates how the variable's current solution deviates from its root solution, thus integrating a historical perspective to guide present decisions. The decision to round up or down is dynamically determined based on a blend of these factors, ensuring that each rounding action is contextually optimized.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial score penalization for dual feasibility
    score = -10 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)
    
    # Update score based on calculated proximity and variable characteristics
    score += proximity_score
    score += 10 * candsol  # Contribution of the variable to the current solution
    score -= 5 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Influence of pseudo-costs
    score += 15 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Relative contribution to the objective function
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty for locks
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Weight for influence in the constraint matrix
    
    # Additional score adjustments for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5
    
    # Incorporating the root solution comparison
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    
    return score, roundup

    score = -15 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0

    # Determining the rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    # Adjusting score based on variable importance and constraints
    score += proximity_score
    score += 8 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score -= 4 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Minimize pseudo costs
    score += 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty
    score += 1.5 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    if isBinary:

This function assesses the fractional part of a variable to decide on rounding up or down, prioritizing proximity to integer boundaries. def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Encourage exploration by penalizing dual rounding feasibility
    score = -10 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0

    # Decision on rounding direction based on fractional part of the solution
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        base_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Higher score for rounding up when closer to the next integer
    else:
        roundup = False
        base_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Higher score for rounding down when closer to the lower integer
    
    # Calculating the total score considering various aspects
    score += base_score
    score += 5 * candsol  # Influence of the current solution value
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Adjust score based on pseudo costs
    score += 1.5 * (obj / objnorm)  # Normalized objective function value impact
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalize based on locking restrictions
    score += 1 * nNonz  # Factor in the number of non-zero entries in the constraint matrix

    # Enhance the score for binary variables significantly
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Increase the weight for binary variables

    # Apply adjustments based on root node comparison
    adjustment = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 3
    score += adjustment if roundup else -adjustment

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False
    
    # Penalize if rounding down/up may not be feasible
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5
    
    # Higher score for variables with lower fractional parts
    score += 1 / (candsfrac + 1)
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher solution values
    score += candsol
    
    # Higher score for variables with more locks for rounding
    score += (nlocksdown + nlocksup) / 10
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher objective function values
    score += obj
    
    # Higher score for variables with greater deviation from the objective function's norm
    score += abs(objnorm - obj)
    
    # Higher score for variables with higher pseudo cost values
    score += max(pscostdown, pscostup)
    
    # Higher score for variables with more nonzero entries
    score += nNonz
    
    # Higher score for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 1
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the sign of the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible to encourage exploration
    score = -10 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0

    # Emphasize proximity to integer values
    base_score = 20 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += base_score

    # Incorporate current solution value and pseudo costs
    score += candsol * 10
    score -= (pscostup if mayroundup else pscostdown)

    # Consider objective function influence normalized by objective norm
    score += 10 * (obj / objnorm)

    # Adjust based on lock numbers and variable's impact in the constraint matrix
    score -= 5 * (nlocksdown if not mayroundup else nlocksup)
    score += 3 * nNonz

    # Enhance scoring for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Adjust based on difference from root node values to favor stability
    adjustment = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5
    score += adjustment if mayroundup else -adjustment

    # Decide rounding direction based on feasibility and closeness to integer
    roundup = (mayroundup and candsfrac >= 0.5) or (mayrounddown and not mayroundup)

    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalize ambivalence to foster decisive actions

    # Prioritize exploration by favoring variables further from integrality
    base_exploration = 15 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5) * 2)
    score += base_exploration

    # Consider variable's contribution to solution value and pseudo-cost impact
    score += 7 * candsol  # Value contribution to current solution
    score -= 3 * (pscostdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else pscostup)  # Cost of rounding

    roundup = score > 0

    score = 0

    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Focus on variables further from being an integer
    base_proximity = 20 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5) * 2)
    score += base_proximity

    # Incorporate the LP relaxation solution value of the variable
    current_value_impact = 10 * candsol
    score += current_value_impact

    # Objective function contribution, adjusted for scaling
    objective_contribution = 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))

    score += pseudo_cost_penalty

    # Lock penalties to favor less constrained rounding decisions

    score += lock_penalty

    deviation_score = 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    score += deviation_score

    # Add impact based on constraint participation
    constraint_impact = 3 * nNonz
    score += constraint_impact

    # Special consideration for binary variables
    if isBinary:

        score -= 8
    
    # Calculate base score from the fractional part, preferring values far from integrality
    score += 10 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5) * 2)
    
    # Include the impact of the variable's current solution value
    score += 5 * candsol
    
    # Adjust based on the objective function's contribution, normalized
    score += 7 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Avoid division by zero
    
    # Include penalties based on pseudo costs to minimize objective increase
    score -= 5 * (pscostdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else pscostup)
    
    # Penalize based on the number of locks to encourage changes
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else nlocksup)
    
    # Increase score for variables significantly differing from their root node values
    score += 3 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    
    # Add a factor for non-zero count in the constraint matrix to encourage affecting more constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz
    
    # Decide rounding direction based on score threshold
    roundup = True if score > 20 else False
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function, named "myheurdiving," is designed to optimize decision-making in MILP diving heuristics by integrating multiple factors that influence variable selection. It prioritizes variables based on their fractional value, contribution to the objective function, and constraints. The function heavily penalizes variables if both rounding directions are feasible to limit unnecessary exploration. It also incorporates pseudo costs and proximity to integer values to refine the scoring. A unique aspect is its adjustment for binary variables and the comparison to the root node solution, providing a comprehensive evaluation framework for MILP problems.

- Penalizes both rounding directions being feasible
- Prioritizes variables closer to integer values
- Considers contribution to objective function and constraints
- Integrates pseudo costs and variable's involvement in constraints
- Adjusts for binary variables and proximity to root node solution

</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Penalize for increased exploration possibilities

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    score += proximity_score

    # Adjust score based on variable's contribution to solution and objective
    score += 10 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score += 12 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance

    # Include pseudo costs to influence rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Account for lock implications on variable rounding
    score -= 2.5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty

    # Prefer variables with higher involvement in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    # Bonus for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.4

    # Adjustment based on comparison to root node solution
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The `myheurdiving` function designed here evaluates variables for the diving heuristic in MILP by considering both feasibility of rounding directions and the variable's potential impact on the solution quality. It initially penalizes variables that can be rounded in both directions, encouraging exploration of less straightforward solutions. The function calculates a base score based on the proximity of the variable to its nearest integer, giving more weight to values closer to an integer. It incorporates adjustments for the variable's influence in the constraint matrix, its effect on the objective function when rounded, and its behavior in previous solutions. Additional considerations include penalties based on pseudo costs which reflect the expected increase in the objective function due to rounding. Special emphasis is given to binary variables, recognizing their significant role in decision-making within MILP solutions. The decision to round up or down is influenced by the feasibility, the proximity to the nearest integer, and the past performance of similar actions, aiming to stabilize and enhance the quality of the heuristic solution.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Increase penalty for dual rounding feasibility to foster exploration
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        base_score = 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding up when closer to next integer
    else:
        roundup = False
        base_score = 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding down when closer to lower integer
    
    # Build the score from various factors
    score += base_score
    score += 10 * candsol  # Weigh in the current solution value
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Incorporate pseudo costs to reflect impact on objective
    score += 2 * (obj / objnorm)  # Normalize influence by objective function norm
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalize based on locks, which restrict flexibility
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Consider the influence in the constraint matrix

    # Extra scoring for binary variables due to their binary nature
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables

    # Adjustment based on comparison with root node values
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new `myheurdiving` function is designed to enhance decision-making in MILP's diving heuristics by integrating a balanced scoring system that considers both penalties and rewards. It penalizes variables that allow both rounding options, encouraging exploration of less flexible variables for faster convergence. The function rewards proximity to the nearest integer, amplifies the score based on the variable's contribution to the objective function, and dynamically adjusts for locking and pseudo-costs. A stability factor is included to account for the variable's consistency across LP solutions. Special attention is given to binary variables due to their decisive nature in determining solution feasibility. The rounding direction not only depends on the fractional value but also on the outcome of the calculated score, enabling adaptive decisions based on current context.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial scoring based on rounding flexibility
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalty for double rounding possibility

    # Decision for rounding direction based on the fractional part and overall score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Increase score as the value approaches 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Increase score as the value approaches 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Fallback rounding decision

    # Add scores from various factors
    score += 10 * candsol  # Positive impact from the variable's solution value
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty adjusted by locking direction
    score += 5 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Objective contribution normalized
    score -= 3 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Pseudo-cost penalty based on direction
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Consider the influence of non-zero constraints

    # Handling binary variables with enhanced focus
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Stronger weight if the variable is binary
    score += 5 * (rootsolval - candsol if roundup else candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` evaluates the desirability of rounding a fractional variable up or down in a MILP problem. It uses a detailed scoring system that accounts for the feasibility of rounding directions, the proximity to the nearest integer, the current solution value, and the variable's impact on the objective function. This function further incorporates the variable's pseudo costs, the number of non-zero constraints it appears in, and special consideration for binary variables, which often have pivotal roles in the solution's structure. By penalizing infeasibility and considering the root node's relaxation solution, this function aims to balance exploratory rounding with stability and solution quality, fostering an informed decision-making process in the diving heuristic.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False

    # Encourage exploration by penalizing inability to round
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5

    # Proximity to nearest integer influences rounding direction and score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up for closeness to next integer
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down for closeness to previous integer

    # Incorporating various influences on the MILP solution
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2  # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    if roundup:
        score += max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Conservative scoring with maximum pseudo cost
    else:
        score -= max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Penalty based on the higher pseudo cost

    # Influence of the variable in the constraint matrix
    score += nNonz * 2  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence

    # Special treatment for binary variables and adjustment based on locking
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    score -= (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup) * 3  # Adjust score based on locks

    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Positive adjustment if rounding up aligns with root solution
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Negative adjustment if rounding down diverges from root solution

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    roundup = False

    # Penalize dual feasibility to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10

        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up when closer to upper bound
    else:

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer

    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to encourage exploration
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 5

    # Influence of proximity to integer, higher proximity leads to stronger consideration for rounding
    proximity_bonus = 20 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if roundup else -proximity_bonus

    # Adjust score based on the solution value and the normalized impact on the objective function
    score += 10 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 5

    # Adjust for pseudo costs, which reflect the historical cost of rounding decisions
    if roundup:
        score -= pscostup
    else:
        score -= pscostdown

    # Consider the variable's constraints, with locks indicating less flexibility
    score -= 2 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)
    score += 3 * nNonz  # Reward variables that influence more constraints

    # Additional adjustments for binary variables due to their discrete nature
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5

    # Adjust for discrepancy between current solution and root node solution value
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 10
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy

    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are possible to increase exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 5

    # Base score from the fractional part, target values far from 0.5
    score += 10 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5) * 2)

    # Influence of the current LP solution value
    score += 3 * candsol

    # Objective function contribution, normalized to avoid extreme effects
    score += 4 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Prevent division by zero

    # Penalize based on pseudo costs, prefer minimal cost direction
    score -= 2 * (pscostdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else pscostup)

    # Penalize based on the number of locks to promote variability
    score -= 1 * (nlocksdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else nlocksup)

    # Reward deviation from root node solution to encourage new explorations
    score += 2 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    # Reward influence on multiple constraints
    score += 1 * nNonz

    # Adjust the score threshold dynamically if the variable is binary
    score_threshold = 15 if isBinary else 10

    # Rounding direction decided by score threshold
    roundup = True if score > score_threshold else False

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function, named "myheurdiving," is designed to optimize decision-making in MILP diving heuristics by integrating multiple factors that influence variable selection. It prioritizes variables based on their fractional value, contribution to the objective function, and constraints. The function heavily penalizes variables if both rounding directions are feasible to limit unnecessary exploration. It also incorporates pseudo costs and proximity to integer values to refine the scoring. A unique aspect is its adjustment for binary variables and the comparison to the root node solution, providing a comprehensive evaluation framework for MILP problems.

- Penalizes both rounding directions being feasible
- Prioritizes variables closer to integer values
- Considers contribution to objective function and constraints
- Integrates pseudo costs and variable's involvement in constraints
- Adjusts for binary variables and proximity to root node solution

</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Penalize for increased exploration possibilities

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    score += proximity_score

    # Adjust score based on variable's contribution to solution and objective
    score += 10 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score += 12 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance

    # Include pseudo costs to influence rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Account for lock implications on variable rounding
    score -= 2.5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty

    # Prefer variables with higher involvement in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    # Bonus for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.4

    # Adjustment based on comparison to root node solution
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The `myheurdiving` function is an innovative scoring mechanism tailored for the MILP diving heuristic, focusing on both maximizing objective function contributions and fostering exploration in decision making. The function evaluates the potential of rounding decisions based on the proximity of the fractional part of a variable to the nearest integer, the importance of the variable in the LP relaxation, and the variable's pseudo costs. The balance between the objective contribution and the cost of changing the variable's LP value plays a pivotal role. Furthermore, the function emphasizes exploration by penalizing scenarios where both rounding up and down are feasible, promoting diverse paths in the solution landscape. Special consideration is given to binary variables and the variable's influence in the constraint matrix, enhancing the strategic depth of rounding decisions. The function dynamically adjusts based on the root node solutions, ensuring adaptability and relevance across different stages of the MILP solving process.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    roundup = False

    # Penalize dual feasibility to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Rounding direction influenced by fractional part proximity to the nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up when closer to upper bound
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down when closer to lower bound

    # Account for solution value, objective function contribution, and pseudo costs
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Penalize based on pseudo costs

    # Adjust score considering the variable's objective contribution and stability
    score += 2 * (obj / objnorm)  # Normalize by objective function norm
    score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Stability check against root node values

    # Modify the score based on variable locks, indicating constraint tightness
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)

    # Adjust score based on non-zero entries, reflecting variable influence
    score += 2 * nNonz

    # Binary variables get additional consideration due to their high impact
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new `myheurdiving` function is designed to enhance decision-making in MILP's diving heuristics by integrating a balanced scoring system that considers both penalties and rewards. It penalizes variables that allow both rounding options, encouraging exploration of less flexible variables for faster convergence. The function rewards proximity to the nearest integer, amplifies the score based on the variable's contribution to the objective function, and dynamically adjusts for locking and pseudo-costs. A stability factor is included to account for the variable's consistency across LP solutions. Special attention is given to binary variables due to their decisive nature in determining solution feasibility. The rounding direction not only depends on the fractional value but also on the outcome of the calculated score, enabling adaptive decisions based on current context.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial scoring based on rounding flexibility
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalty for double rounding possibility

    # Decision for rounding direction based on the fractional part and overall score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Increase score as the value approaches 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Increase score as the value approaches 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Fallback rounding decision

    # Add scores from various factors
    score += 10 * candsol  # Positive impact from the variable's solution value
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty adjusted by locking direction
    score += 5 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Objective contribution normalized
    score -= 3 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Pseudo-cost penalty based on direction
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Consider the influence of non-zero constraints

    # Handling binary variables with enhanced focus
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Stronger weight if the variable is binary
    score += 5 * (rootsolval - candsol if roundup else candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor

    return score, roundup
</end_code>
