The new score function `myheurdiving` evaluates the desirability of rounding a fractional variable up or down in a MILP problem, incorporating various factors to guide effective rounding decisions. It penalizes infeasible rounding directions to encourage exploration, considers the proximity to the nearest integer, assesses the variable's impact on constraints and objective function, adjusts scores based on pseudo costs and root solution deviation, and promotes exploration through conservative scoring. The goal is to provide a balanced scoring mechanism that balances feasibility and optimization objectives.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Initial rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer

    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to encourage exploration
    score -= 10 * (not mayrounddown)
    score -= 10 * (not mayroundup)

    # Proximity to integer influences score
    proximity_bonus = 15 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if roundup else -proximity_bonus

    # Solution value and objective function impact
    score += 8 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 6

    # Pseudo costs adjustment
    score -= 0.8 * pscostup if roundup else 0.8 * pscostdown

    # Constraints influence
    score -= 1.5 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)
    score += 2.5 * nNonz

    # Adjustments for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.3

    # Root solution deviation
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 8
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy

    # Additional conservative scoring and exploration encouragement
    score += 0.3 * (mayrounddown + mayroundup)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` evaluates the desirability of rounding a fractional variable up or down in a MILP problem. It uses a detailed scoring system that accounts for the feasibility of rounding directions, the proximity to the nearest integer, the current solution value, and the variable's impact on the objective function. This function further incorporates the variable's pseudo costs, the number of non-zero constraints it appears in, and special consideration for binary variables, which often have pivotal roles in the solution's structure. By penalizing infeasibility and considering the root node's relaxation solution, this function aims to balance exploratory rounding with stability and solution quality, fostering an informed decision-making process in the diving heuristic.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False

    # Encourage exploration by penalizing inability to round
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5

    # Proximity to nearest integer influences rounding direction and score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up for closeness to next integer
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down for closeness to previous integer

    # Incorporating various influences on the MILP solution
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2  # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    if roundup:
        score += max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Conservative scoring with maximum pseudo cost
    else:
        score -= max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Penalty based on the higher pseudo cost

    # Influence of the variable in the constraint matrix
    score += nNonz * 2  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence

    # Special treatment for binary variables and adjustment based on locking
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    score -= (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup) * 3  # Adjust score based on locks

    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Positive adjustment if rounding up aligns with root solution
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Negative adjustment if rounding down diverges from root solution

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are possible to increase exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 5

    # Base score from the fractional part, target values far from 0.5
    score += 10 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5) * 2)

    # Influence of the current LP solution value
    score += 3 * candsol

    # Objective function contribution, normalized to avoid extreme effects
    score += 4 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Prevent division by zero

    # Penalize based on pseudo costs, prefer minimal cost direction
    score -= 2 * (pscostdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else pscostup)

    # Penalize based on the number of locks to promote variability
    score -= 1 * (nlocksdown if candsfrac < 0.5 else nlocksup)

    # Reward deviation from root node solution to encourage new explorations
    score += 2 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    # Reward influence on multiple constraints
    score += 1 * nNonz

    # Adjust the score threshold dynamically if the variable is binary
    score_threshold = 15 if isBinary else 10

    # Rounding direction decided by score threshold
    roundup = True if score > score_threshold else False

    return score, roundup

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Penalize for increased exploration possibilities

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    score += proximity_score

    # Adjust score based on variable's contribution to solution and objective
    score += 10 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score += 12 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance

    # Include pseudo costs to influence rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Account for lock implications on variable rounding
    score -= 2.5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty

    # Prefer variables with higher involvement in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    # Bonus for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.4

    # Adjustment based on comparison to root node solution
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The `myheurdiving` function is an innovative scoring mechanism tailored for the MILP diving heuristic, focusing on both maximizing objective function contributions and fostering exploration in decision making. The function evaluates the potential of rounding decisions based on the proximity of the fractional part of a variable to the nearest integer, the importance of the variable in the LP relaxation, and the variable's pseudo costs. The balance between the objective contribution and the cost of changing the variable's LP value plays a pivotal role. Furthermore, the function emphasizes exploration by penalizing scenarios where both rounding up and down are feasible, promoting diverse paths in the solution landscape. Special consideration is given to binary variables and the variable's influence in the constraint matrix, enhancing the strategic depth of rounding decisions. The function dynamically adjusts based on the root node solutions, ensuring adaptability and relevance across different stages of the MILP solving process.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    roundup = False

    # Penalize dual feasibility to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Rounding direction influenced by fractional part proximity to the nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up when closer to upper bound
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down when closer to lower bound

    # Account for solution value, objective function contribution, and pseudo costs
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Penalize based on pseudo costs

    # Adjust score considering the variable's objective contribution and stability
    score += 2 * (obj / objnorm)  # Normalize by objective function norm
    score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Stability check against root node values

    # Modify the score based on variable locks, indicating constraint tightness
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)

    # Adjust score based on non-zero entries, reflecting variable influence
    score += 2 * nNonz

    # Binary variables get additional consideration due to their high impact
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new `myheurdiving` function is developed to optimize MILP's diving heuristic by evaluating the trade-off between exploration and exploitation effectively. This function integrates a multifaceted scoring mechanism to make decisions based on variable attributes. It penalizes variables that have too much flexibility in rounding, promoting exploration of less frequented paths, and gives precedence to variables closer to integral values, encouraging exploitation of promising regions. Additionally, the function considers variable contribution to the objective, adjusts for locking effects and pseudo-costs, rewards deviation from the root solution, factors in the variable’s involvement in constraints, and prioritizes binary variables due to their typically high impact on the problem’s structure and constraints. The function returns a score, higher being more preferable, and a boolean indicating whether rounding up is recommended.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize for having both rounding options, to enhance exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 15  # Strong penalty for variables with high flexibility

    # Scoring and rounding decision based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Prefer rounding up closer to 1
        roundup = True
    elif mayrounddown:
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Prefer rounding down closer to 0
        roundup = False
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default rounding based on fractional part

    # Objective contribution scaled by normalized objective value
    score += 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Normalize and amplify objective contribution

    # Penalty adjustments for locking and pseudo-costs
    if roundup:
        score -= 10 * pscostup  # Higher pseudo-cost for rounding up reduces score
        score -= 2 * nlocksup  # Locking penalty for rounding up
    else:
        score -= 10 * pscostdown  # Higher pseudo-cost for rounding down reduces score
        score -= 2 * nlocksdown  # Locking penalty for rounding down

    # Reward deviation from root solution to encourage diverse search
    score += 5 * abs(rootsolval - candsol)  # Reward changes from the root solution

    # Consider the variable's influence in constraints
    score += 3 * nNonz  # Reward for variables involved in more constraints

    # Increase score for binary variables due to their pivotal role
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Boost for binary variables

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new `myheurdiving` function is designed to enhance decision-making in MILP's diving heuristics by integrating a balanced scoring system that considers both penalties and rewards. It penalizes variables that allow both rounding options, encouraging exploration of less flexible variables for faster convergence. The function rewards proximity to the nearest integer, amplifies the score based on the variable's contribution to the objective function, and dynamically adjusts for locking and pseudo-costs. A stability factor is included to account for the variable's consistency across LP solutions. Special attention is given to binary variables due to their decisive nature in determining solution feasibility. The rounding direction not only depends on the fractional value but also on the outcome of the calculated score, enabling adaptive decisions based on current context.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial scoring based on rounding flexibility
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalty for double rounding possibility

    # Decision for rounding direction based on the fractional part and overall score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Increase score as the value approaches 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Increase score as the value approaches 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Fallback rounding decision

    # Add scores from various factors
    score += 10 * candsol  # Positive impact from the variable's solution value
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty adjusted by locking direction
    score += 5 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Objective contribution normalized
    score -= 3 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Pseudo-cost penalty based on direction
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Consider the influence of non-zero constraints

    # Handling binary variables with enhanced focus
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Stronger weight if the variable is binary
    score += 5 * (rootsolval - candsol if roundup else candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function, "adaptive_heuristic," integrates multiple factors to guide effective rounding decisions in MILP diving heuristics. It balances exploration and exploitation by penalizing infeasible rounding directions and prioritizing variables near integer values. The function considers the variable's influence on constraints and objective function, adjusts scores based on pseudo costs, and incorporates a root solution deviation factor. Additionally, it rewards variables heavily involved in constraints, adjusts for binary variables, and includes a dynamic scaling mechanism for further refinement.

Key features:
1. Penalizes infeasible rounding directions to promote exploration.
2. Prioritizes variables closer to integer values.
3. Accounts for the variable's impact on constraints and objective function.
4. Adjusts scores based on pseudo costs and root solution deviation.
5. Rewards higher involvement in constraints and adjusts for binary variables.
6. Implements a dynamic scaling factor to refine the scoring process.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Determine initial rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    
    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to encourage exploration
    score -= 10 * (not mayrounddown)
    score -= 10 * (not mayroundup)
    
    # Proximity to integer influences score
    proximity_bonus = 25 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if roundup else -proximity_bonus
    
    # Solution value and objective function impact
    score += 15 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 10
    
    # Pseudo costs adjustment
    score -= 1.0 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)
    
    # Constraints influence
    score -= 2.0 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)
    score += 3.0 * nNonz
    
    # Adjustments for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.2
    
    # Root solution deviation
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 12
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy
    
    # Dynamic scaling factor for refinement
    scaling_factor = 1.1 if roundup else 0.9
    score *= scaling_factor
    
    # Additional conservative scoring and exploration encouragement
    score += 0.7 * (mayrounddown + mayroundup)
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Initial rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer

    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to encourage exploration
    score -= 10 * (not mayrounddown)
    score -= 10 * (not mayroundup)

    # Proximity to integer influences score
    proximity_bonus = 15 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if roundup else -proximity_bonus

    # Solution value and objective function impact
    score += 8 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 6

    # Pseudo costs adjustment
    score -= 0.8 * pscostup if roundup else 0.8 * pscostdown

    # Constraints influence

    score += 2.5 * nNonz

    # Root solution deviation
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 8
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy

    # Additional conservative scoring and exploration encouragement
    score += 0.3 * (mayrounddown + mayroundup)

    return score, roundup

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` evaluates the desirability of rounding a fractional variable up or down in a MILP problem. It uses a detailed scoring system that accounts for the feasibility of rounding directions, the proximity to the nearest integer, the current solution value, and the variable's impact on the objective function. This function further incorporates the variable's pseudo costs, the number of non-zero constraints it appears in, and special consideration for binary variables, which often have pivotal roles in the solution's structure. By penalizing infeasibility and considering the root node's relaxation solution, this function aims to balance exploratory rounding with stability and solution quality, fostering an informed decision-making process in the diving heuristic.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False

    # Encourage exploration by penalizing inability to round
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5

    # Proximity to nearest integer influences rounding direction and score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up for closeness to next integer
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down for closeness to previous integer

    # Incorporating various influences on the MILP solution
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2  # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    if roundup:
        score += max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Conservative scoring with maximum pseudo cost
    else:
        score -= max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Penalty based on the higher pseudo cost

    # Influence of the variable in the constraint matrix
    score += nNonz * 2  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence

    # Special treatment for binary variables and adjustment based on locking
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    score -= (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup) * 3  # Adjust score based on locks

    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Positive adjustment if rounding up aligns with root solution
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Negative adjustment if rounding down diverges from root solution

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to integer

    # Penalize restricted rounding options to foster exploration
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 15
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 15

    # Reward proximity to the nearest integer, with emphasis near rounding decision
    proximity_bonus = 25 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if roundup else -proximity_bonus

    # Evaluate the impact on the objective function scaled by variable's solution value
    score += 10 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 15

    # Incorporate historical performance metrics via pseudo costs
    if roundup:
        score -= pscostup * 1.8
    else:
        score -= pscostdown * 1.8

    # Penalize variables with more locks in the undesired rounding direction
    score -= 8 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)

    # Boost score for potentially impactful variables based on non-zero constraints count
    score += 5 * nNonz

    # Boost score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5

    # Adjust score based on alignment with root node solution values
    root_adjustment = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 25
    score += root_adjustment if roundup else -root_adjustment

    return score, roundup

    score -= 10 * (not mayrounddown)
    score -= 10 * (not mayroundup)
    
    # Proximity to integer influences score
    proximity_bonus = 25 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if roundup else -proximity_bonus
    
    # Solution value and objective function impact
    score += 15 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 10
    
    # Pseudo costs adjustment
    score -= 1.0 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)
    
    # Constraints influence
    score -= 2.0 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)
    score += 3.0 * nNonz
    
    # Adjustments for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.2
    
    # Root solution deviation
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 12
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy
    
    # Dynamic scaling factor for refinement
    scaling_factor = 1.1 if roundup else 0.9
    score *= scaling_factor
    
    # Additional conservative scoring and exploration encouragement
    score += 0.7 * (mayrounddown + mayroundup)
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Initial rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer

    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to encourage exploration
    score -= 10 * (not mayrounddown)
    score -= 10 * (not mayroundup)

    # Proximity to integer influences score
    proximity_bonus = 15 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if roundup else -proximity_bonus

    # Solution value and objective function impact
    score += 8 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 6

    # Pseudo costs adjustment
    score -= 0.8 * pscostup if roundup else 0.8 * pscostdown

    # Constraints influence
    score -= 1.5 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)
    score += 2.5 * nNonz

    # Adjustments for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.3

    # Root solution deviation
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 8
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy

    # Additional conservative scoring and exploration encouragement
    score += 0.3 * (mayrounddown + mayroundup)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` evaluates the desirability of rounding a fractional variable up or down in a MILP problem. It uses a detailed scoring system that accounts for the feasibility of rounding directions, the proximity to the nearest integer, the current solution value, and the variable's impact on the objective function. This function further incorporates the variable's pseudo costs, the number of non-zero constraints it appears in, and special consideration for binary variables, which often have pivotal roles in the solution's structure. By penalizing infeasibility and considering the root node's relaxation solution, this function aims to balance exploratory rounding with stability and solution quality, fostering an informed decision-making process in the diving heuristic.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False

    # Encourage exploration by penalizing inability to round
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5

    # Proximity to nearest integer influences rounding direction and score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up for closeness to next integer
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down for closeness to previous integer

    # Incorporating various influences on the MILP solution
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2  # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    if roundup:
        score += max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Conservative scoring with maximum pseudo cost
    else:
        score -= max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Penalty based on the higher pseudo cost

    # Influence of the variable in the constraint matrix
    score += nNonz * 2  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence

    # Special treatment for binary variables and adjustment based on locking
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    score -= (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup) * 3  # Adjust score based on locks

    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Positive adjustment if rounding up aligns with root solution
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Negative adjustment if rounding down diverges from root solution

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    score -= 3 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)

<start_des>
The new score function, named "myheurdiving," is designed to optimize decision-making in MILP diving heuristics by integrating multiple factors that influence variable selection. It prioritizes variables based on their fractional value, contribution to the objective function, and constraints. The function heavily penalizes variables if both rounding directions are feasible to limit unnecessary exploration. It also incorporates pseudo costs and proximity to integer values to refine the scoring. A unique aspect is its adjustment for binary variables and the comparison to the root node solution, providing a comprehensive evaluation framework for MILP problems.

- Penalizes both rounding directions being feasible
- Prioritizes variables closer to integer values
- Considers contribution to objective function and constraints
- Integrates pseudo costs and variable's involvement in constraints
- Adjusts for binary variables and proximity to root node solution

</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Penalize for increased exploration possibilities

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    score += proximity_score

    # Adjust score based on variable's contribution to solution and objective
    score += 10 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score += 12 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance

    # Include pseudo costs to influence rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Account for lock implications on variable rounding
    score -= 2.5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty

    # Prefer variables with higher involvement in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    # Bonus for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.4

    # Adjustment based on comparison to root node solution
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function, named "myheurdiving," aims to refine the diving heuristic's effectiveness by integrating exploration and exploitation strategies. def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    
    # Exploration penalty for dual rounding feasibility
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 20  # Increase the penalty to enhance exploration

    # Rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        base_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding up when closer to next integer
    else:
        roundup = False
        base_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding down when closer to lower integer

    # Compute the score considering various factors
    score += base_score
    score += 15 * candsol  # Increase weight of current solution value
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown) * 2  # Double the impact of pseudo costs
    score += 5 * (obj / objnorm)  # Adjust objective function influence
    score -= 5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Adjust penalties for locks
    score += 3 * nNonz  # Adjust influence in constraints

    # Special handling for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Adjust scoring multiplier for binary variables

    # Root node value comparison adjustment
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 10  # Increase impact for rounding up
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 10  # Increase impact for rounding down

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    roundup = False

    # Penalize dual feasibility to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Rounding direction influenced by fractional part proximity to the nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up when closer to upper bound
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down when closer to lower bound

    # Account for solution value, objective function contribution, and pseudo costs
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Penalize based on pseudo costs

    # Adjust score considering the variable's objective contribution and stability
    score += 2 * (obj / objnorm)  # Normalize by objective function norm
    score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Stability check against root node values

    # Modify the score based on variable locks, indicating constraint tightness
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)

    # Adjust score based on non-zero entries, reflecting variable influence
    score += 2 * nNonz

    # Binary variables get additional consideration due to their high impact
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    return score, roundup

The new `myheurdiving` function aims to prioritize variables in the MILP diving heuristic by considering various factors such as the possibility of rounding, proximity to integer values, objective contribution, locking mechanisms, pseudo-costs, deviation from root solutions, number of constraints involved, and the binary nature of variables. It encourages exploration by penalizing high flexibility in rounding options, while also favoring variables closer to integer values and those with significant objective contributions. It penalizes locking and pseudo-costs to avoid getting stuck in local optima and rewards deviation from root solutions to promote diverse search paths. Additionally, it emphasizes binary variables due to their substantial impact on the MILP structure.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize high flexibility in rounding options to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Determine the rounding direction based on proximity to the nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Bias towards rounding up when closer to 1
    elif candsfrac < 0.5 and mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Bias towards rounding down when closer to 0
    else:
        # Default to rounding up if exactly at 0.5 and rounding up is possible
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup

    # Amplify the objective contribution, normalized
    score += 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))

    # Adjust score based on locking and pseudo-costs
    if roundup:
        score -= 5 * pscostup  # Pseudo-cost penalty for rounding up
        score -= 3 * nlocksup  # Locking penalty for rounding up
    else:
        score -= 5 * pscostdown  # Pseudo-cost penalty for rounding down
        score -= 3 * nlocksdown  # Locking penalty for rounding down

    # Reward deviation from the root solution to encourage a diverse search path
    score += 5 * abs(rootsolval - candsol)  # Reward significant deviations from the root

    # Increase score for variables involved in multiple constraints
    score += 3 * nNonz  # Reward involvement in more constraints

    # Special treatment for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Increase importance of binary variables in scoring

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize for having both rounding options, to enhance exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 15  # Strong penalty for variables with high flexibility

    # Scoring and rounding decision based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Prefer rounding up closer to 1
        roundup = True
    elif mayrounddown:
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Prefer rounding down closer to 0
        roundup = False
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default rounding based on fractional part

    # Objective contribution scaled by normalized objective value
    score += 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Normalize and amplify objective contribution

    # Penalty adjustments for locking and pseudo-costs
    if roundup:
        score -= 10 * pscostup  # Higher pseudo-cost for rounding up reduces score
        score -= 2 * nlocksup  # Locking penalty for rounding up
    else:
        score -= 10 * pscostdown  # Higher pseudo-cost for rounding down reduces score
        score -= 2 * nlocksdown  # Locking penalty for rounding down

    # Reward deviation from root solution to encourage diverse search
    score += 5 * abs(rootsolval - candsol)  # Reward changes from the root solution

    # Consider the variable's influence in constraints
    score += 3 * nNonz  # Reward for variables involved in more constraints

    # Increase score for binary variables due to their pivotal role
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Boost for binary variables

    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial scoring based on rounding flexibility
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalty for double rounding possibility

    # Decision for rounding direction based on the fractional part and overall score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Increase score as the value approaches 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Increase score as the value approaches 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Fallback rounding decision

    # Add scores from various factors
    score += 10 * candsol  # Positive impact from the variable's solution value
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty adjusted by locking direction
    score += 5 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Objective contribution normalized

    if isBinary:

    score += 5 * (rootsolval - candsol if roundup else candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor

    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to promote exploration
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 10
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Calculate proximity bonus, with a higher bonus for closeness to the nearest integer
    proximity_bonus = 30 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -proximity_bonus

    # Incorporate the variable's contribution to the solution quality
    score += 20 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 10

    if candsfrac >= 0.5:

    score += 5 * nNonz

    score -= 0.5 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)
    
    # Constraints influence
    score -= 3 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence
    
    # Special treatment for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    
    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function, named "myheurdiving," is designed to optimize decision-making in MILP diving heuristics by integrating multiple factors that influence variable selection. It prioritizes variables based on their fractional value, contribution to the objective function, and constraints. The function heavily penalizes variables if both rounding directions are feasible to limit unnecessary exploration. It also incorporates pseudo costs and proximity to integer values to refine the scoring. A unique aspect is its adjustment for binary variables and the comparison to the root node solution, providing a comprehensive evaluation framework for MILP problems.

- Penalizes both rounding directions being feasible
- Prioritizes variables closer to integer values
- Considers contribution to objective function and constraints
- Integrates pseudo costs and variable's involvement in constraints
- Adjusts for binary variables and proximity to root node solution

</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Penalize for increased exploration possibilities

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    score += proximity_score

    # Adjust score based on variable's contribution to solution and objective
    score += 10 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score += 12 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance

    # Include pseudo costs to influence rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Account for lock implications on variable rounding
    score -= 2.5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty

    # Prefer variables with higher involvement in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    # Bonus for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.4

    # Adjustment based on comparison to root node solution
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function, named "myheurdiving," aims to refine the diving heuristic's effectiveness by integrating exploration and exploitation strategies. It evaluates rounding directions based on the fractional value and encourages diversity by penalizing the dual rounding feasibility of variables. The function incorporates multiple variables such as solution value, objective contribution, pseudo costs, and locks to balance immediate profit against longer-term strategy. Special adjustments are made for binary variables and differences from the root node values, emphasizing their impact on the decision-making process. This approach optimizes the choice of variables to branch on during the MILP solving process.
<end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    
    # Exploration penalty for dual rounding feasibility
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 20  # Increase the penalty to enhance exploration

    # Rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        base_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding up when closer to next integer
    else:
        roundup = False
        base_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding down when closer to lower integer

    # Compute the score considering various factors
    score += base_score
    score += 15 * candsol  # Increase weight of current solution value
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown) * 2  # Double the impact of pseudo costs
    score += 5 * (obj / objnorm)  # Adjust objective function influence
    score -= 5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Adjust penalties for locks
    score += 3 * nNonz  # Adjust influence in constraints

    # Special handling for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Adjust scoring multiplier for binary variables

    # Root node value comparison adjustment
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 10  # Increase impact for rounding up
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 10  # Increase impact for rounding down

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The `myheurdiving` function is an innovative scoring mechanism tailored for the MILP diving heuristic, focusing on both maximizing objective function contributions and fostering exploration in decision making. The function evaluates the potential of rounding decisions based on the proximity of the fractional part of a variable to the nearest integer, the importance of the variable in the LP relaxation, and the variable's pseudo costs. The balance between the objective contribution and the cost of changing the variable's LP value plays a pivotal role. Furthermore, the function emphasizes exploration by penalizing scenarios where both rounding up and down are feasible, promoting diverse paths in the solution landscape. Special consideration is given to binary variables and the variable's influence in the constraint matrix, enhancing the strategic depth of rounding decisions. The function dynamically adjusts based on the root node solutions, ensuring adaptability and relevance across different stages of the MILP solving process.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    roundup = False

    # Penalize dual feasibility to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Rounding direction influenced by fractional part proximity to the nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up when closer to upper bound
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down when closer to lower bound

    # Account for solution value, objective function contribution, and pseudo costs
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Penalize based on pseudo costs

    # Adjust score considering the variable's objective contribution and stability
    score += 2 * (obj / objnorm)  # Normalize by objective function norm
    score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Stability check against root node values

    # Modify the score based on variable locks, indicating constraint tightness
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)

    # Adjust score based on non-zero entries, reflecting variable influence
    score += 2 * nNonz

    # Binary variables get additional consideration due to their high impact
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function aims to balance exploration and exploitation by penalizing potential dual rounding directions, encouraging more exploration. It considers the fractional part of the variable's solution and its closeness to integral values. Additionally, it evaluates the variable's contribution to the objective, its locking behavior, and stability across LP solutions. Pseudo-cost adjustments and the number of non-zero constraints also influence the score. Binary variables receive special treatment to prioritize their decisive nature, and stability is measured by the deviation from the root node solution.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initialize the score
    score = 0

    # Penalize potential dual rounding directions for exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 7

    # Decision for rounding direction based on fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 18 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Enhancing the score as it approaches 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 18 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Enhancing the score as it approaches 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default rounding decision when conditions are ambiguous

    # Influence score based on the variable's current solution value
    score += 8 * candsol

    # Penalty based on locking behavior
    score -= 1.5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)

    # Normalized contribution of the variable to the objective
    score += 3 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))

    # Pseudo-cost adjustment based on rounding direction
    score -= 2.5 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)

    # Impact from the number of non-zero constraints
    score += 1.5 * nNonz

    # Special handling for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.8  # Increase the weight significantly if the variable is binary

    # Stability factor considering the deviation from the root node solution
    score += 3 * (rootsolval - candsol if roundup else candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function, named "myheurdiving," adopts a balanced approach to evaluate variable candidates for rounding, considering multiple factors such as rounding flexibility, proximity to integer values, solution values, objective function contribution, pseudo costs, deviation from the root solution, non-zero entries, and binary nature of variables. <start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    
    # Penalize variables with high flexibility in rounding to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 15  # Strong penalty for variables with both rounding options
    
    # Proximity to integer influences score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Prefer rounding up closer to 1
        roundup = True
    elif mayrounddown:
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Prefer rounding down closer to 0
        roundup = False
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default rounding based on fractional part
    
    # Reward higher solution values
    score += 10 * candsol
    
    # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2
    
    # Adjust score based on pseudo costs
    if roundup:
        score -= 0.5 * pscostup  # Higher pseudo-cost for rounding up reduces score
        score -= 2 * nlocksup  # Locking penalty for rounding up
    else:
        score -= 0.5 * pscostdown  # Higher pseudo-cost for rounding down reduces score
        score -= 2 * nlocksdown  # Locking penalty for rounding down
    
    # Reward deviation from root solution to encourage diverse search
    score += 5 * abs(rootsolval - candsol)  # Reward changes from the root solution
    
    # Consider variable's influence in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Reward for variables involved in more constraints
    
    # Boost score for binary variables due to their pivotal role
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial scoring based on rounding flexibility
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalty for double rounding possibility

    # Decision for rounding direction based on the fractional part and overall score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Increase score as the value approaches 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Increase score as the value approaches 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Fallback rounding decision

    # Add scores from various factors
    score += 10 * candsol  # Positive impact from the variable's solution value
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty adjusted by locking direction
    score += 5 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Objective contribution normalized
    score -= 3 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Pseudo-cost penalty based on direction
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Consider the influence of non-zero constraints

    # Handling binary variables with enhanced focus
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Stronger weight if the variable is binary
    score += 5 * (rootsolval - candsol if roundup else candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor

    return score, roundup

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` incorporates a comprehensive approach to scoring variables in MILP diving heuristics. It prioritizes exploration by penalizing cases where rounding down or up may not be feasible, encouraging a broader search space. It evaluates the proximity to integer boundaries, considering both the fractional part of the solution and the solution value itself. Additionally, it accounts for the variable's contribution to the objective function and its significance in constraint equations. Historical rounding costs are factored in through pseudo costs, influencing the decision-making process. Special attention is given to binary variables due to their significant impact on solutions. Furthermore, it adjusts the score based on the deviation from the root node solution, aligning rounding decisions with past successful solutions. The function also considers the flexibility in variable behavior indicated by locks, providing adaptability in the rounding process.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    
    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to promote exploration
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 10
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Calculate proximity bonus, with a higher bonus for closeness to the nearest integer
    proximity_bonus = 30 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -proximity_bonus

    # Incorporate the variable's contribution to the solution quality
    score += 20 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 10

    # Account for historical cost of rounding decisions using pseudo costs
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        score -= 2 * pscostup
    else:
        score -= 2 * pscostdown

    # Consider the variable's participation in constraints, adding score based on its prevalence
    score += 5 * nNonz

    # Special handling for binary variables due to their significant impact on solutions
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Adjust for the deviation from the root node solution, aligning rounding with previous best solutions
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 15
    score += root_discrepancy if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -root_discrepancy

    # Modify score based on locks, indicating flexibility in variable behavior
    score -= 5 * (nlocksup if candsfrac >= 0.5 else nlocksdown)

    return score, candsfrac >= 0.5
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` evaluates the desirability of rounding a fractional variable up or down in a MILP problem. It uses a detailed scoring system that accounts for the feasibility of rounding directions, the proximity to the nearest integer, the current solution value, and the variable's impact on the objective function. This function further incorporates the variable's pseudo costs, the number of non-zero constraints it appears in, and special consideration for binary variables, which often have pivotal roles in the solution's structure. By penalizing infeasibility and considering the root node's relaxation solution, this function aims to balance exploratory rounding with stability and solution quality, fostering an informed decision-making process in the diving heuristic.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False

    # Encourage exploration by penalizing inability to round
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5

    # Proximity to nearest integer influences rounding direction and score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up for closeness to next integer
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down for closeness to previous integer

    # Incorporating various influences on the MILP solution
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2  # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    if roundup:
        score += max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Conservative scoring with maximum pseudo cost
    else:
        score -= max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Penalty based on the higher pseudo cost

    # Influence of the variable in the constraint matrix
    score += nNonz * 2  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence

    # Special treatment for binary variables and adjustment based on locking
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    score -= (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup) * 3  # Adjust score based on locks

    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Positive adjustment if rounding up aligns with root solution
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Negative adjustment if rounding down diverges from root solution

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function prioritizes exploration by penalizing variables with both rounding options available. It rewards proximity to integer values, favors variables contributing more to the objective, and considers pseudo-costs, locking effects, and non-zero entries. Additionally, it promotes solution diversity by factoring in the deviation from the root solution. Binary variables are emphasized due to their critical role in feasibility.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    
    # Penalize availability of both rounding directions to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 20
    
    # Decision making for rounding direction based on the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Reward approaching 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Reward approaching 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default to rounding up if closer to 1
    
    # Influence of other variables
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher LP solution values
    score += (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6)) * 5  # Normalize and scale objective contribution
    
    # Adjust scores based on pseudo-costs and locking
    if roundup:
        score -= pscostup  # Deduct pseudo-cost for rounding up
        score -= nlocksup * 5  # Deduct more for locks when rounding up
    else:
        score -= pscostdown  # Deduct pseudo-cost for rounding down
        score -= nlocksdown * 5  # Deduct for locks when rounding down
    
    # Consider deviation from the root solution to promote diversity
    score += 10 * abs(rootsolval - candsol)
    
    # Include the influence of constraints where the variable appears
    score += 3 * nNonz
    
    # Enhance the importance of binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Increase the score if the variable is binary
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

score:[1.508267006707953, 12.616705307747889]
<start_des>
The `myheurdiving` function is designed to optimize the selection and rounding direction of variables in MILP diving heuristics. It integrates a multi-faceted evaluation approach that emphasizes a variable's feasibility for rounding, proximity to integer values, influence on the objective function, and implications from constraint relationships. The function penalizes variables when both rounding directions are feasible to enhance the exploration of solution space. The score increases with the variable’s proximity to the nearest integer, its contribution to the current LP solution, and its relevance to the objective function. Pseudo-costs are integrated to reflect the potential cost of rounding decisions. The function also considers locking effects, which indicate how constraint satisfaction changes with variable rounding, and it gives special consideration to binary variables, boosting their score due to their dichotomous nature. The function dynamically adjusts the scoring based on the stability between the current solution and the root node solution, aiming for consistent decision-making across LP relaxations.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Encourage exploration by penalizing

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Higher score as closer to 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Higher score as closer to 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default rounding decision if no feasible direction
        proximity_score = 0

    score += proximity_score

    # Enhance score based on variable's contribution and objective relevance
    score += 12 * candsol  # Current solution value influence
    score += 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Scaled objective contribution

    # Factor in pseudo costs for rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Adjust score based on locking effects
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty for constraint tightness

    # Prefer variables with significant constraint involvement
    score += 3 * nNonz  # Weightage for non-zero entries in the constraint matrix

    # Extra scoring boost for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Boost for binary variables due to critical decision impact

    # Stability adjustment comparing current solution with root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += 7 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor for rounding up
    else:
        score -= 7 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor for rounding down

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function, named "myheurdiving," adopts a balanced approach to evaluate variable candidates for rounding, considering multiple factors such as rounding flexibility, proximity to integer values, solution values, objective function contribution, pseudo costs, deviation from the root solution, non-zero entries, and binary nature of variables. It aims to incentivize exploration while ensuring feasibility and encouraging diverse search strategies.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    
    # Penalize variables with high flexibility in rounding to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 15  # Strong penalty for variables with both rounding options
    
    # Proximity to integer influences score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Prefer rounding up closer to 1
        roundup = True
    elif mayrounddown:
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Prefer rounding down closer to 0
        roundup = False
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default rounding based on fractional part
    
    # Reward higher solution values
    score += 10 * candsol
    
    # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2
    
    # Adjust score based on pseudo costs
    if roundup:
        score -= 0.5 * pscostup  # Higher pseudo-cost for rounding up reduces score
        score -= 2 * nlocksup  # Locking penalty for rounding up
    else:
        score -= 0.5 * pscostdown  # Higher pseudo-cost for rounding down reduces score
        score -= 2 * nlocksdown  # Locking penalty for rounding down
    
    # Reward deviation from root solution to encourage diverse search
    score += 5 * abs(rootsolval - candsol)  # Reward changes from the root solution
    
    # Consider variable's influence in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Reward for variables involved in more constraints
    
    # Boost score for binary variables due to their pivotal role
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new `myheurdiving` function is designed to enhance decision-making in MILP's diving heuristics by integrating a balanced scoring system that considers both penalties and rewards. It penalizes variables that allow both rounding options, encouraging exploration of less flexible variables for faster convergence. The function rewards proximity to the nearest integer, amplifies the score based on the variable's contribution to the objective function, and dynamically adjusts for locking and pseudo-costs. A stability factor is included to account for the variable's consistency across LP solutions. Special attention is given to binary variables due to their decisive nature in determining solution feasibility. The rounding direction not only depends on the fractional value but also on the outcome of the calculated score, enabling adaptive decisions based on current context.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial scoring based on rounding flexibility
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalty for double rounding possibility

    # Decision for rounding direction based on the fractional part and overall score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Increase score as the value approaches 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Increase score as the value approaches 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Fallback rounding decision

    # Add scores from various factors
    score += 10 * candsol  # Positive impact from the variable's solution value
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty adjusted by locking direction
    score += 5 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Objective contribution normalized
    score -= 3 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Pseudo-cost penalty based on direction
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Consider the influence of non-zero constraints

    # Handling binary variables with enhanced focus
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Stronger weight if the variable is binary
    score += 5 * (rootsolval - candsol if roundup else candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` incorporates a comprehensive approach to scoring variables in MILP diving heuristics. It prioritizes exploration by penalizing cases where rounding down or up may not be feasible, encouraging a broader search space. It evaluates the proximity to integer boundaries, considering both the fractional part of the solution and the solution value itself. Additionally, it accounts for the variable's contribution to the objective function and its significance in constraint equations. Historical rounding costs are factored in through pseudo costs, influencing the decision-making process. Special attention is given to binary variables due to their significant impact on solutions. Furthermore, it adjusts the score based on the deviation from the root node solution, aligning rounding decisions with past successful solutions. The function also considers the flexibility in variable behavior indicated by locks, providing adaptability in the rounding process.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    
    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to promote exploration
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 10
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Calculate proximity bonus, with a higher bonus for closeness to the nearest integer
    proximity_bonus = 30 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -proximity_bonus

    # Incorporate the variable's contribution to the solution quality
    score += 20 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 10

    # Account for historical cost of rounding decisions using pseudo costs
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        score -= 2 * pscostup
    else:
        score -= 2 * pscostdown

    # Consider the variable's participation in constraints, adding score based on its prevalence
    score += 5 * nNonz

    # Special handling for binary variables due to their significant impact on solutions
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Adjust for the deviation from the root node solution, aligning rounding with previous best solutions
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 15
    score += root_discrepancy if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -root_discrepancy

    # Modify score based on locks, indicating flexibility in variable behavior
    score -= 5 * (nlocksup if candsfrac >= 0.5 else nlocksdown)

    return score, candsfrac >= 0.5
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` evaluates the desirability of rounding a fractional variable up or down in a MILP problem. It uses a detailed scoring system that accounts for the feasibility of rounding directions, the proximity to the nearest integer, the current solution value, and the variable's impact on the objective function. This function further incorporates the variable's pseudo costs, the number of non-zero constraints it appears in, and special consideration for binary variables, which often have pivotal roles in the solution's structure. By penalizing infeasibility and considering the root node's relaxation solution, this function aims to balance exploratory rounding with stability and solution quality, fostering an informed decision-making process in the diving heuristic.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False

    # Encourage exploration by penalizing inability to round
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5

    # Proximity to nearest integer influences rounding direction and score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up for closeness to next integer
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down for closeness to previous integer

    # Incorporating various influences on the MILP solution
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2  # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    if roundup:
        score += max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Conservative scoring with maximum pseudo cost
    else:
        score -= max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Penalty based on the higher pseudo cost

    # Influence of the variable in the constraint matrix
    score += nNonz * 2  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence

    # Special treatment for binary variables and adjustment based on locking
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    score -= (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup) * 3  # Adjust score based on locks

    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Positive adjustment if rounding up aligns with root solution
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Negative adjustment if rounding down diverges from root solution

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function, named "myheurdiving," is designed to optimize decision-making in MILP diving heuristics by integrating multiple factors that influence variable selection. It prioritizes variables based on their fractional value, contribution to the objective function, and constraints. The function heavily penalizes variables if both rounding directions are feasible to limit unnecessary exploration. It also incorporates pseudo costs and proximity to integer values to refine the scoring. A unique aspect is its adjustment for binary variables and the comparison to the root node solution, providing a comprehensive evaluation framework for MILP problems.

- Penalizes both rounding directions being feasible
- Prioritizes variables closer to integer values
- Considers contribution to objective function and constraints
- Integrates pseudo costs and variable's involvement in constraints
- Adjusts for binary variables and proximity to root node solution

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Penalize for increased exploration possibilities

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    score += proximity_score

    # Adjust score based on variable's contribution to solution and objective
    score += 10 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score += 12 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance

    # Include pseudo costs to influence rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Account for lock implications on variable rounding
    score -= 2.5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty

    # Prefer variables with higher involvement in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    # Bonus for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.4

    # Adjustment based on comparison to root node solution
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup

    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 20  # Increase the penalty to enhance exploration

    # Rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        base_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding up when closer to next integer
    else:
        roundup = False
        base_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Stronger emphasis on rounding down when closer to lower integer

    # Compute the score considering various factors
    score += base_score
    score += 15 * candsol  # Increase weight of current solution value
    score -= (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown) * 2  # Double the impact of pseudo costs

    if roundup:

    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 10  # Increase impact for rounding down

The new score function, named "myheurdiving," adopts a balanced approach to evaluate variable candidates for rounding, considering multiple factors such as rounding flexibility, proximity to integer values, solution values, objective function contribution, pseudo costs, deviation from the root solution, non-zero entries, and binary nature of variables. It aims to incentivize exploration while ensuring feasibility and encouraging diverse search strategies.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    
    # Penalize variables with high flexibility in rounding to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 15  # Strong penalty for variables with both rounding options
    
    # Proximity to integer influences score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Prefer rounding up closer to 1
        roundup = True
    elif mayrounddown:
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Prefer rounding down closer to 0
        roundup = False
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default rounding based on fractional part
    
    # Reward higher solution values
    score += 10 * candsol
    
    # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2
    
    # Adjust score based on pseudo costs
    if roundup:
        score -= 0.5 * pscostup  # Higher pseudo-cost for rounding up reduces score
        score -= 2 * nlocksup  # Locking penalty for rounding up
    else:
        score -= 0.5 * pscostdown  # Higher pseudo-cost for rounding down reduces score
        score -= 2 * nlocksdown  # Locking penalty for rounding down
    
    # Reward deviation from root solution to encourage diverse search
    score += 5 * abs(rootsolval - candsol)  # Reward changes from the root solution
    
    # Consider variable's influence in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Reward for variables involved in more constraints
    
    # Boost score for binary variables due to their pivotal role
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>
