<start_des>
The new `myheurdiving` function is designed to enhance decision-making in MILP's diving heuristics by integrating a balanced scoring system that considers both penalties and rewards. It penalizes variables that allow both rounding options, encouraging exploration of less flexible variables for faster convergence. The function rewards proximity to the nearest integer, amplifies the score based on the variable's contribution to the objective function, and dynamically adjusts for locking and pseudo-costs. A stability factor is included to account for the variable's consistency across LP solutions. Special attention is given to binary variables due to their decisive nature in determining solution feasibility. The rounding direction not only depends on the fractional value but also on the outcome of the calculated score, enabling adaptive decisions based on current context.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial scoring based on rounding flexibility
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalty for double rounding possibility

    # Decision for rounding direction based on the fractional part and overall score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Increase score as the value approaches 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Increase score as the value approaches 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Fallback rounding decision

    # Add scores from various factors
    score += 10 * candsol  # Positive impact from the variable's solution value
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty adjusted by locking direction
    score += 5 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Objective contribution normalized
    score -= 3 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Pseudo-cost penalty based on direction
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Consider the influence of non-zero constraints

    # Handling binary variables with enhanced focus
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Stronger weight if the variable is binary
    score += 5 * (rootsolval - candsol if roundup else candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` incorporates a comprehensive approach to scoring variables in MILP diving heuristics. It prioritizes exploration by penalizing cases where rounding down or up may not be feasible, encouraging a broader search space. It evaluates the proximity to integer boundaries, considering both the fractional part of the solution and the solution value itself. Additionally, it accounts for the variable's contribution to the objective function and its significance in constraint equations. Historical rounding costs are factored in through pseudo costs, influencing the decision-making process. Special attention is given to binary variables due to their significant impact on solutions. Furthermore, it adjusts the score based on the deviation from the root node solution, aligning rounding decisions with past successful solutions. The function also considers the flexibility in variable behavior indicated by locks, providing adaptability in the rounding process.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    
    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to promote exploration
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 10
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Calculate proximity bonus, with a higher bonus for closeness to the nearest integer
    proximity_bonus = 30 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -proximity_bonus

    # Incorporate the variable's contribution to the solution quality
    score += 20 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 10

    # Account for historical cost of rounding decisions using pseudo costs
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        score -= 2 * pscostup
    else:
        score -= 2 * pscostdown

    # Consider the variable's participation in constraints, adding score based on its prevalence
    score += 5 * nNonz

    # Special handling for binary variables due to their significant impact on solutions
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Adjust for the deviation from the root node solution, aligning rounding with previous best solutions
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 15
    score += root_discrepancy if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -root_discrepancy

    # Modify score based on locks, indicating flexibility in variable behavior
    score -= 5 * (nlocksup if candsfrac >= 0.5 else nlocksdown)

    return score, candsfrac >= 0.5
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` evaluates the desirability of rounding a fractional variable up or down in a MILP problem. It uses a detailed scoring system that accounts for the feasibility of rounding directions, the proximity to the nearest integer, the current solution value, and the variable's impact on the objective function. This function further incorporates the variable's pseudo costs, the number of non-zero constraints it appears in, and special consideration for binary variables, which often have pivotal roles in the solution's structure. By penalizing infeasibility and considering the root node's relaxation solution, this function aims to balance exploratory rounding with stability and solution quality, fostering an informed decision-making process in the diving heuristic.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False

    # Encourage exploration by penalizing inability to round
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5

    # Proximity to nearest integer influences rounding direction and score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up for closeness to next integer
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down for closeness to previous integer

    # Incorporating various influences on the MILP solution
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2  # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    if roundup:
        score += max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Conservative scoring with maximum pseudo cost
    else:
        score -= max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Penalty based on the higher pseudo cost

    # Influence of the variable in the constraint matrix
    score += nNonz * 2  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence

    # Special treatment for binary variables and adjustment based on locking
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    score -= (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup) * 3  # Adjust score based on locks

    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Positive adjustment if rounding up aligns with root solution
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Negative adjustment if rounding down diverges from root solution

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Determine initial rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    
    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to encourage exploration
    score -= 0.7 * (mayrounddown or mayroundup)  # Penalize if both rounding options available
    
    # Proximity to integer influences score
    proximity_bonus = 20 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if roundup else -proximity_bonus
    
    # Solution value and objective function impact
    score += 12 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 3  # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    
    # Pseudo costs adjustment
    score -= 0.6 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)
    
    # Constraints influence
    score -= 4 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)
    score += 3 * nNonz  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence
    
    # Special treatment for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.8  # Enhance the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    
    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 7
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy
    
    return score, roundup

The new scoring function, `myheurdiving`, is designed to efficiently evaluate variables within the context of a MILP diving heuristic. The function emphasizes exploration and penalizes scenarios where both rounding directions are possible, to encourage deeper search into less explored branches. Proximity to the nearest integer is highly valued, promoting choices that lead to feasible integer solutions faster. The function also incorporates the variable's contribution to the overall objective, historical rounding costs, and locking effects, which help in maintaining feasibility and optimality. The effect of binary variables is amplified, reflecting their critical role in feasibility. The function's output provides both the score and the preferred rounding direction, essential for making strategic decisions during the diving process.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    
    # Encourage exploration by penalizing the availability of both rounding options
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 20

    # Reward proximity to nearest integer, higher bonus for closer proximity
    proximity_bonus = 30 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -proximity_bonus

    # Add the variable's contribution to the objective quality
    score += 20 * candsol + (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6)) * 10

    # Incorporate historical pseudo-costs to reflect past rounding impacts
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        score -= 2 * pscostup
    else:
        score -= 2 * pscostdown

    # Account for locking effects, reducing score for higher locks
    score -= 5 * (nlocksup if candsfrac >= 0.5 else nlocksdown)

    # Promote diversity by considering deviation from the root solution
    score += 10 * abs(rootsolval - candsol)

    # Factor in the variable's presence in multiple constraints
    score += 3 * nNonz

    # Increase score for binary variables due to their critical role
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5

    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function, named "myheurdiving," is designed to optimize decision-making in MILP diving heuristics by integrating multiple factors that influence variable selection. It prioritizes variables based on their fractional value, contribution to the objective function, and constraints. The function heavily penalizes variables if both rounding directions are feasible to limit unnecessary exploration. It also incorporates pseudo costs and proximity to integer values to refine the scoring. A unique aspect is its adjustment for binary variables and the comparison to the root node solution, providing a comprehensive evaluation framework for MILP problems.

- Penalizes both rounding directions being feasible
- Prioritizes variables closer to integer values
- Considers contribution to objective function and constraints
- Integrates pseudo costs and variable's involvement in constraints
- Adjusts for binary variables and proximity to root node solution

</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Penalize for increased exploration possibilities

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    score += proximity_score

    # Adjust score based on variable's contribution to solution and objective
    score += 10 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score += 12 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance

    # Include pseudo costs to influence rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Account for lock implications on variable rounding
    score -= 2.5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty

    # Prefer variables with higher involvement in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    # Bonus for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.4

    # Adjustment based on comparison to root node solution
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new `myheurdiving` function is crafted to enhance the decision-making process in MILP by evaluating the feasibility and strategic value of rounding a variable's fractional solution up or down. This function assesses variables based on several criteria including their proximity to integer values, their influence on the objective function, and their historical rounding performance. It integrates a dynamic scoring system that factors in the feasibility of rounding directions, the variable's participation in constraints, and its relevance to the objective function, adjusting for both exploration needs and potential rounding benefits. The function also accounts for the variable's deviation from the root solution and prioritizes binary variables due to their critical impact on the problem's complexity and solution structure. The scoring dynamically adapts to encourage exploration where options are open and precision where rounding direction can significantly influence feasibility.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac > 0.5  # Favor rounding up if exactly halfway or more

    # Apply penalty if both rounding options are available, to encourage exploring more complex paths
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Increase score based on variable's contribution to objective and its normalized objective impact
    score += 20 * candsol + 10 * (obj / objnorm)

    # Adjust score based on proximity to nearest integer, favoring the nearest rounding option
    proximity_bonus = 30 * min(candsfrac, 1 - candsfrac)
    score += proximity_bonus if roundup else -proximity_bonus

    # Incorporate pseudo costs to reflect past performance of rounding decisions
    score -= 5 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)

    # Enhance score for variables involved in more constraints, suggesting higher strategic value
    score += 5 * nNonz

    # Special handling for binary variables to emphasize their significant impact
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Consider deviation from root solution, influencing more conservative or aggressive rounding
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 15
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy

    # Deduct points based on locks, which restrict flexibility in changing the rounding direction
    score -= 2 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    
    # Penalize availability of both rounding directions to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 20
    
    # Decision making for rounding direction based on the fractional part
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Reward approaching 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Reward approaching 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default to rounding up if closer to 1
    
    # Influence of other variables
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher LP solution values
    score += (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6)) * 5  # Normalize and scale objective contribution
    
    # Adjust scores based on pseudo-costs and locking
    if roundup:
        score -= pscostup  # Deduct pseudo-cost for rounding up
        score -= nlocksup * 5  # Deduct more for locks when rounding up
    else:
        score -= pscostdown  # Deduct pseudo-cost for rounding down
        score -= nlocksdown * 5  # Deduct for locks when rounding down
    
    # Consider deviation from the root solution to promote diversity
    score += 10 * abs(rootsolval - candsol)
    
    # Include the influence of constraints where the variable appears
    score += 3 * nNonz
    
    # Enhance the importance of binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Increase the score if the variable is binary
    
    return score, roundup

The `myheurdiving` function is designed to optimize the selection and rounding direction of variables in MILP diving heuristics. It integrates a multi-faceted evaluation approach that emphasizes a variable's feasibility for rounding, proximity to integer values, influence on the objective function, and implications from constraint relationships. The function penalizes variables when both rounding directions are feasible to enhance the exploration of solution space. The score increases with the variable’s proximity to the nearest integer, its contribution to the current LP solution, and its relevance to the objective function. Pseudo-costs are integrated to reflect the potential cost of rounding decisions. The function also considers locking effects, which indicate how constraint satisfaction changes with variable rounding, and it gives special consideration to binary variables, boosting their score due to their dichotomous nature. The function dynamically adjusts the scoring based on the stability between the current solution and the root node solution, aiming for consistent decision-making across LP relaxations.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Encourage exploration by penalizing

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Higher score as closer to 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Higher score as closer to 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default rounding decision if no feasible direction
        proximity_score = 0

    score += proximity_score

    # Enhance score based on variable's contribution and objective relevance
    score += 12 * candsol  # Current solution value influence
    score += 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Scaled objective contribution

    # Factor in pseudo costs for rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Adjust score based on locking effects
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty for constraint tightness

    # Prefer variables with significant constraint involvement
    score += 3 * nNonz  # Weightage for non-zero entries in the constraint matrix

    # Extra scoring boost for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Boost for binary variables due to critical decision impact

    # Stability adjustment comparing current solution with root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += 7 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor for rounding up
    else:
        score -= 7 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor for rounding down

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The function also considers the flexibility in variable behavior indicated by locks, providing adaptability in the rounding process.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    
    # Penalize inability to round in certain directions to promote exploration
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 10
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 10

    # Calculate proximity bonus, with a higher bonus for closeness to the nearest integer
    proximity_bonus = 30 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -proximity_bonus

    # Incorporate the variable's contribution to the solution quality
    score += 20 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 10

    # Account for historical cost of rounding decisions using pseudo costs
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        score -= 2 * pscostup
    else:
        score -= 2 * pscostdown

    # Consider the variable's participation in constraints, adding score based on its prevalence
    score += 5 * nNonz

    # Special handling for binary variables due to their significant impact on solutions
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Adjust for the deviation from the root node solution, aligning rounding with previous best solutions
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 15
    score += root_discrepancy if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -root_discrepancy

    # Modify score based on locks, indicating flexibility in variable behavior
    score -= 5 * (nlocksup if candsfrac >= 0.5 else nlocksdown)

    return score, candsfrac >= 0.5
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` evaluates the desirability of rounding a fractional variable up or down in a MILP problem. It uses a detailed scoring system that accounts for the feasibility of rounding directions, the proximity to the nearest integer, the current solution value, and the variable's impact on the objective function. This function further incorporates the variable's pseudo costs, the number of non-zero constraints it appears in, and special consideration for binary variables, which often have pivotal roles in the solution's structure. By penalizing infeasibility and considering the root node's relaxation solution, this function aims to balance exploratory rounding with stability and solution quality, fostering an informed decision-making process in the diving heuristic.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False

    # Encourage exploration by penalizing inability to round
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5

    # Proximity to nearest integer influences rounding direction and score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up for closeness to next integer
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down for closeness to previous integer

    # Incorporating various influences on the MILP solution
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2  # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    if roundup:
        score += max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Conservative scoring with maximum pseudo cost
    else:
        score -= max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Penalty based on the higher pseudo cost

    # Influence of the variable in the constraint matrix
    score += nNonz * 2  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence

    # Special treatment for binary variables and adjustment based on locking
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    score -= (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup) * 3  # Adjust score based on locks

    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Positive adjustment if rounding up aligns with root solution
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Negative adjustment if rounding down diverges from root solution

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Determines the initial rounding direction

    # Penalty for having both rounding options to promote more variable exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 0.3

    # Adding proximity to the nearest integer score
    proximity = 20 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity if roundup else -proximity

    # Influence on the objective function and solution quality
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 3  # Normalize contribution to objective
    
    # Adjusting for pseudo costs
    score -= 0.7 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)
    
    # Constraint influence through locks
    score -= 4 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)

    # Adding a score for the number of nonzero entries to promote constraints satisfaction
    score += 3 * nNonz

    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Increase importance for binary variables
    
    # Deviation from the root solution
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 7
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy
    
    return score, roundup

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Start with a penalty if both rounding directions are possible to encourage exploration
    score = -10 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0

    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac > 0.5  # Prefer rounding up if fraction is over 0.5

    # Proximity to integer: closer to 0.5, stronger the preference
    proximity_score = 20 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5)) * 2
    score += proximity_score if roundup else -proximity_score

    # Impact on objective function normalized by objective norm
    obj_contribution = 15 * obj / (objnorm if objnorm != 0 else 1)  # Normalize and guard against zero division
    score += obj_contribution

    # Pseudo costs reflect historical rounding costs
    pseudo_cost_impact = -3 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)
    score += pseudo_cost_impact

    # Number of non-zero constraints indicates influence on the problem
    score += 3 * nNonz

    # Binary variables have a higher impact on the solution's feasibility
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Increase importance of binary variables

    # Stability factor for deviations from the root node solution
    stability_factor = 8 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    score -= stability_factor if roundup else stability_factor

    # Locks imply constraint tightness, affecting variable flexibility
    locks_penalty = -1 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)
    score += locks_penalty

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

- Penalizes both rounding directions being feasible
- Prioritizes variables closer to integer values
- Considers contribution to objective function and constraints

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Penalize for increased exploration possibilities

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    score += proximity_score

    # Adjust score based on variable's contribution to solution and objective
    score += 10 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score += 12 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance

    # Include pseudo costs to influence rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Account for lock implications on variable rounding
    score -= 2.5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty

    # Prefer variables with higher involvement in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    # Bonus for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.4

    # Adjustment based on comparison to root node solution
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Encourage exploration by penalizing

    score += 12 * candsol  # Current solution value influence

    # Calculate proximity bonus, with a higher bonus for closeness to the nearest integer
    proximity_bonus = 30 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -proximity_bonus

    # Incorporate the variable's contribution to the solution quality
    score += 20 * candsol + (obj / objnorm) * 10

    # Account for historical cost of rounding decisions using pseudo costs
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        score -= 2 * pscostup
    else:
        score -= 2 * pscostdown

    # Consider the variable's participation in constraints, adding score based on its prevalence
    score += 5 * nNonz

    # Special handling for binary variables due to their significant impact on solutions
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Adjust for the deviation from the root node solution, aligning rounding with previous best solutions
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 15
    score += root_discrepancy if candsfrac >= 0.5 else -root_discrepancy

    # Modify score based on locks, indicating flexibility in variable behavior
    score -= 5 * (nlocksup if candsfrac >= 0.5 else nlocksdown)

    return score, candsfrac >= 0.5
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function `myheurdiving` evaluates the desirability of rounding a fractional variable up or down in a MILP problem. It uses a detailed scoring system that accounts for the feasibility of rounding directions, the proximity to the nearest integer, the current solution value, and the variable's impact on the objective function. This function further incorporates the variable's pseudo costs, the number of non-zero constraints it appears in, and special consideration for binary variables, which often have pivotal roles in the solution's structure. By penalizing infeasibility and considering the root node's relaxation solution, this function aims to balance exploratory rounding with stability and solution quality, fostering an informed decision-making process in the diving heuristic.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False

    # Encourage exploration by penalizing inability to round
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5

    # Proximity to nearest integer influences rounding direction and score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        score += 15 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Favor rounding up for closeness to next integer
    else:
        score += 15 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Favor rounding down for closeness to previous integer

    # Incorporating various influences on the MILP solution
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 2  # Scale contribution to the objective by its norm
    if roundup:
        score += max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Conservative scoring with maximum pseudo cost
    else:
        score -= max(pscostup, pscostdown)  # Penalty based on the higher pseudo cost

    # Influence of the variable in the constraint matrix
    score += nNonz * 2  # Add score based on non-zero constraints presence

    # Special treatment for binary variables and adjustment based on locking
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2  # Double the score for binary variables due to their decisive nature
    score -= (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup) * 3  # Adjust score based on locks

    # Consider the discrepancy from root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Positive adjustment if rounding up aligns with root solution
    else:
        score -= abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 5  # Negative adjustment if rounding down diverges from root solution

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Determines the initial rounding direction

    # Penalty for having both rounding options to promote more variable exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 0.3

    # Adding proximity to the nearest integer score
    proximity = 20 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity if roundup else -proximity

    # Influence on the objective function and solution quality
    score += 10 * candsol  # Reward higher solution values
    score += (obj / objnorm) * 3  # Normalize contribution to objective
    
    # Adjusting for pseudo costs
    score -= 0.7 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)
    
    # Constraint influence through locks
    score -= 4 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)

    # Adding a score for the number of nonzero entries to promote constraints satisfaction
    score += 3 * nNonz
    
    # Boost for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Increase importance for binary variables
    
    # Deviation from the root solution
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 7
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy
    
    return score, roundup

    score = 0.0
    roundup = candsfrac > 0.5  # Favor rounding up if the fraction is greater than 0.5

    # Penalty for flexibility in rounding options, promoting exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 15

    # Bonus for contribution to the objective, normalized by objective vector norm
    score += 25 * candsol + 15 * (obj / objnorm)

    # Proximity to the nearest integer for the fractional part, applying a bonus or penalty based on the rounding direction
    proximity_bonus = 40 * min(candsfrac, 1 - candsfrac)

    # Adjustments based on pseudo costs which reflect the variable's historical rounding performance
    score -= 10 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)

    score += 10 * nNonz

    if isBinary:
        score *= 2.5

    # Impact of deviation from the root solution, adjusted for rounding direction
    root_discrepancy = abs(candsol - rootsolval) * 20
    score += root_discrepancy if roundup else -root_discrepancy

    # Locks impact, reducing score based on restrictions in rounding flexibility
    score -= 3 * (nlocksup if roundup else nlocksdown)

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0
    # Decide rounding direction primarily based on the fractional part
    roundup = candsfrac > 0.5

    # Penalize if both rounding options are feasible to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 50

    # Reward or penalize based on proximity to the nearest integer
    proximity_bonus = 30 * (1 - abs(candsfrac - 0.5))
    score += proximity_bonus if roundup else -proximity_bonus

    # Adjust score based on the variable's contribution to the objective function
    obj_contribution = 20 * candsol + 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))
    score += obj_contribution

    # Incorporate pseudo costs to reflect past performance and risk
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= 10 * pseudo_cost

    # Add score for involvement in multiple constraints
    score += 5 * nNonz

    # Enhance score for binary variables due to their strategic impact
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Factor in stability between current solution and root node solution
    root_stability = 15 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)
    score += root_stability if roundup else -root_stability

    # Penalize based on the number of locks to reflect reduced flexibility
    score -= 5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Penalize for increased exploration possibilities

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)
    else:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)

    score += proximity_score

    # Adjust score based on variable's contribution to solution and objective
    score += 10 * candsol  # Value contribution in current LP solution
    score += 12 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-5))  # Objective function relevance

    # Include pseudo costs to influence rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Account for lock implications on variable rounding
    score -= 2.5 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Constraint tightness penalty

    # Prefer variables with higher involvement in constraints
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Influence of the variable in constraints

    # Bonus for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.4

    # Adjustment based on comparison to root node solution
    score += 5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval) if roundup else -5 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)

    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0

    # Penalize if both rounding directions are feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 10  # Encourage exploration by penalizing

    # Determine rounding direction based on proximity to nearest integer
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        proximity_score = 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Higher score as closer to 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        proximity_score = 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Higher score as closer to 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Default rounding decision if no feasible direction
        proximity_score = 0

    score += proximity_score

    # Enhance score based on variable's contribution and objective relevance
    score += 12 * candsol  # Current solution value influence
    score += 10 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Scaled objective contribution

    # Factor in pseudo costs for rounding decisions
    pseudo_cost = pscostup if roundup else pscostdown
    score -= pseudo_cost

    # Adjust score based on locking effects
    score -= 3 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty for constraint tightness

    # Prefer variables with significant constraint involvement
    score += 3 * nNonz  # Weightage for non-zero entries in the constraint matrix

    # Extra scoring boost for binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Boost for binary variables due to critical decision impact

    # Stability adjustment comparing current solution with root node solution
    if roundup:
        score += 7 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor for rounding up
    else:
        score -= 7 * abs(candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor for rounding down

    return score, roundup

The function rewards proximity to the nearest integer, amplifies the score based on the variable's contribution to the objective function, and dynamically adjusts for locking and pseudo-costs. def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Initial scoring based on rounding flexibility
    score = -5 if mayrounddown and mayroundup else 0  # Penalty for double rounding possibility

    # Decision for rounding direction based on the fractional part and overall score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5 and mayroundup:
        roundup = True
        score += 20 * (candsfrac - 0.5)  # Increase score as the value approaches 1
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
        score += 20 * (0.5 - candsfrac)  # Increase score as the value approaches 0
    else:
        roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5  # Fallback rounding decision

    # Add scores from various factors
    score += 10 * candsol  # Positive impact from the variable's solution value
    score -= 2 * (nlocksdown if not roundup else nlocksup)  # Penalty adjusted by locking direction
    score += 5 * (obj / (objnorm + 1e-6))  # Objective contribution normalized
    score -= 3 * (pscostup if roundup else pscostdown)  # Pseudo-cost penalty based on direction
    score += 2 * nNonz  # Consider the influence of non-zero constraints

    # Handling binary variables with enhanced focus
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5  # Stronger weight if the variable is binary
    score += 5 * (rootsolval - candsol if roundup else candsol - rootsolval)  # Stability factor

    return score, roundup
